## Description

#### What type of PR is this?

/kind feature
/area skills

#### What this PR does / why we need it?

Adds a `maintainers-review` agent skill. It turns the requests that Kueue approvers
repeat in PR reviews into a checklist that contributors' agents apply to their own diff
before pushing, so reviews can focus on design instead of the same simple comments
(unrelated diff churn, missing gate-off tests, release-note format, stale comments, tests
that pass without the fix, and so on).

- `cmd/experimental/skills/maintainers-review/SKILL.md`: how to use the skill and a
  60-item checklist (`MR-A1` to `MR-J4`), with `[simple]` marking items an agent can fix
  without design judgment.
- `cmd/experimental/skills/maintainers-review/rules.md`: each rule with its reason, who
  raises it, and one to three precedents that link to the exact review comment
  (`#discussion_r…`, `#issuecomment-…`, `#pullrequestreview-…`) with a verbatim quote, so
  every rule can be checked. It also lists where reviewers disagree and how that was
  resolved, and old asks that no longer apply.
- `cmd/experimental/skills/maintainers-review/reviewers/<login>.md`: one profile per
  reviewer (mimowo, tenzen-y, sohankunkerkar, mbobrovskyi, alculquicondor, ahg-g,
  gabesaba, kerthcet) with the full set of rules and precedents.
- `AGENTS.md`: a short "Maintainers" section that points agents to the checklist before
  pushing; `cmd/experimental/skills/README.md`: one row for the skill.
- All files, profiles included, pass the skillsaw content rules. The profiles quote review
  comments verbatim, so the lines where a quote trips a rule (for example "try to" or
  "ideally" in a reviewer's own words) carry a
  `<!-- skillsaw-disable-next-line <rule> -->` comment naming the exact rule. The second
  commit holds the rewording and these suppressions, so it can be reverted on its own if
  you prefer a file-level exclude for the profiles.

#### Useful notes for your reviewer

No behavior change (docs/skills only).

How the rules were built:
- Source: the `raw/` folder of [reruno/kueue-llm-wiki](https://github.com/reruno/kueue-llm-wiki),
  an export of kubernetes-sigs/kueue PRs and issues with all their comments. From it I took
  every PR and issue comment by these eight current and emeritus approvers up to 2026-11-24,
  read with the surrounding thread (bots excluded). The wiki pages in that repo were not
  used, only the raw threads.
- Each profile went through six rounds of a verification pass (does each quote support
  the rule in its thread, is it a pattern of at least 3 PRs or an explicit policy, is it
  still current against today's code, linters, contribution guidelines and newer
  maintainer comments) and a search pass for recurring asks the profile missed.
- Conflicts follow the current maintainers and the newest comments, then today's code and
  guidelines (for example `DescribeTable` only for webhook/CRD validation tests, errors
  compared by identity, log levels from `logging_policy.md`, `new(expr)` over `ptr.To`).
- Every quote was matched against the reviewer's comments through the GitHub API, which
  also produced the exact comment links; quotes from PRs since deleted from GitHub were
  dropped.

The reviewer profiles describe review patterns of named people. If any of you prefer your
profile to be dropped or reworded, I will do that.

Checks:
- `make verify-skills-lint`: passed.
- `make verify`: passed.

AI disclosure: this PR was prepared with AI assistance (Claude Code). I reviewed the
content.

- **cmd/experimental/skills/maintainers-review/reviewers/sohankunkerkar.md:170** by [@coderabbitai[bot]](https://github.com/apps/coderabbitai) — 2026-11-25T11:31:22Z
```diff
@@ -0,0 +1,430 @@
+# sohankunkerkar: review profile
+
+In this data, sohankunkerkar comments now and then from 2025-03, becomes a regular
+reviewer from 2025-11, and first uses `/approve` on 2026-06-15 (PR #12279). Since then they
+approve often (113 `/approve`, 200 `/lgtm`). The review data has 729 threads (539 PRs, 190
+issues) and 2,766 of their own entries. 1,894 of those are on other people's threads: 896
+inline review comments, 770 conversation comments and 228 review bodies. The other 872 are
+replies on their own 176 threads. Their activity grows over time. On others' threads they
+wrote 54 entries in 2025-Q4, about 270 per quarter in 2026-Q1 and Q2, 549 in Q3 and 740 in
+Q4 (to 11-24). Where their inline comments land on others' PRs: KEPs 39% (350), test code
+17% (151), controllers/jobframework/job integrations about 13%, queue/scheduler caches
+about 10%, and scheduler, TAS, MultiKueue, webhooks, APIs and docs 1–3% each. DRA is the
+main subject. Most of their long threads are DRA KEPs and fixes (#13601, #14130, #10973,
+#9742, #10320, #18632). After that come elastic jobs/workload slices, WaitForPodsReady,
+partial preemption (#14589), TAS, custom metric labels and MultiKueue.
+
+## What they care about most
+
+Shares are rough: they come from keyword stats over about 1,500 substantive comments, and one
+comment can hit several themes. See Method.
+
+1. **Behavior traced through the code: edge cases, what really happens, races, and whether every stuck state has an exit and a message the user can act on** (the biggest share; about 22% of comments are questions, and most of those are "what happens when…").
+2. **KEP rigor and readability**: claims about today's code checked against main, scope, interactions, gates, validation, Beta/GA criteria, Alternatives, user stories that still hold, terminology, tables, each point said once, no implementation detail, known gaps written into Risks/Drawbacks, and the KEP kept in sync with the code (39% of their inline comments are on KEPs; prose and structure comments about 7% in 2026-H2).
+3. **Tests that actually prove the change**: missing cases (every new branch and each integration sharing the code), gate-off cases, testing through the real entry point with production-shaped inputs, and running the change with a mutation ("still passes without the fix") (17% of inline comments are on test files; test requests about 3%; mutation checks rise in H2).
+4. **Feature gates and upgrade or backport safety**: behavior with the gate on and off, crashloops on upgrade, cherry-pick shape (about 8% mention gates; about 3% upgrade or backport).
+5. **Release process hygiene**: release notes (they write 32 release-note blocks for authors), CP requests, rebases, `/kind`, PR title and description, `Fixes` vs `Part of` (CP/backport about 11% of entries in H2).
+6. **Simplicity and reuse**: drop dead or redundant code, reuse existing helpers, wrappers and patterns (about 7% "drop/remove", about 6% "reuse/already does").
+7. **Accurate names and comments**: names that match behavior and local patterns, comments that are not stale or wordy (about 6% naming, about 4% comment wording).
+8. **Log verbosity and performance on hot paths** (about 2–4% each; logging mostly in H1, before `logging_policy.md` fixed the levels in 2026-10).
+
+## Rules for authors
+
+Precedent format: `[PR #N](url) kind date [@ path:line]: "exact quote"`. Each quote is an
+exact, contiguous substring of the comment body in the review data.
+
+### Naming and style
+
+- **[simple] Make related names agree: constructor with type, option with its sibling option, function name with KEP term, new helper with the codebase's naming pattern.** They read mismatched names as bugs in the making. Why: about 8 comments in 7 PRs (#9232, #9323, #10156, #14130, #14154, #14589).
+  - [PR #14130](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14130#discussion_r4109128806) inline 2026-09-25 @ pkg/dra/claims.go:153: "nit: `chargeForFirstAvailable`, to match the KEP and the error messages?"
+  - [PR #10156](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10156#discussion_r3025568341) inline 2026-04-02 @ pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go:137: "nit: `WithAdmissionFairSharingConfig` to be consistent with the localqueue_controller"
+  - [PR #9232](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9232#discussion_r2807029838) inline 2026-02-14 @ pkg/cache/queue/inadmissible_workloads.go:223: "The constructor says "Reconciler", type says "Requeuer". Pick one."
+  - [PR #14154](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14154#discussion_r3776095286) inline 2026-08-13 @ pkg/workload/workload.go:696: "nit: I'd change the naming to `subtractReplacedRequestsFrom` to match the codebase pattern"
+- **[simple] Name a function, helper or test case for what it actually does, and rename it when its behavior changes.** A stale name misleads the next reader. Test case names count too: say the resource, the outcome and the error kind the case really has. So do log, error and field-error messages: say exactly what the code did ("failed to persist … condition", not "cleared" when nothing is cleared), word a new message like its siblings in the file, and pass the dereferenced value, not a pointer, as `field.Invalid`'s bad value. `coding_guidelines.md` only asks for "human-readable messages". Why: about 9 comments in 9 PRs (#8963, #9894, #14562, #15983, #16217, #16253, #16723, #17526, #18632); messages add 4 comments in 3 PRs (#9808, #14562, #15888 twice).
+  - [PR #17526](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17526#discussion_r4408227953) inline 2026-10-29 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go:1479: "This now writes the spec and removes the annotation, so the name is misleading. Maybe `SyncWaitForPodsReady`?"
+  - [PR #15983](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15983#discussion_r4071512436) inline 2026-09-22 @ pkg/dra/extended_resources.go:218: "nit: These aren't charges or the pod's real containers, they're stand-ins carrying restartPolicy and requests so PodRequests applies sidecar semantics, so I'd call it `containersForPodRequests`"
+  - [PR #18632](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/18632#discussion_r4577111319) inline 2026-11-15 @ pkg/dra/claims_test.go:1138: "nit: This case expects a rejection, so could the name say "is rejected as unmapped" instead of "is charged"?"
+  - [PR #15888](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15888#discussion_r4065434376) inline 2026-09-21 @ pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go:207: "nit: nothing is cleared here as we set `Requeued=True` with `DRAResourcesResolved`. Maybe "failed to persist DRA resources resolved condition"."
+- **Don't give an existing Kueue term a new meaning, and don't coin new terms.** Overloaded words confuse users and readers. Why: about 5 comments in 4 PRs (#13601, #14227, #14589, #17500).
+  - [PR #14589](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14589#discussion_r4396895200) inline 2026-10-28 @ keps/975-partial-preemption/README.md:252: "Kueue already uses "reclaim" for `reclaimWithinCohort` (taking back borrowed quota)"
+  - [PR #14227](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14227#discussion_r4168326368) inline 2026-10-02 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/manage/observability/configure_custom_metric_labels.md:82: ""Workload control metadata" is a new term, it isn't used anywhere else in the repo."
+- **[simple] Fix typos and drop stray or leftover lines before pushing.** They point these out with `s/x/y/g` or suggestion blocks. The `misspell` linter only catches common misspellings, so identifier and test-name typos still reach review. Why: about 8 comments in 7 PRs (#8579, #9232, #9808, #9894, #13601, #17804).
+  - [PR #9894](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9894#discussion_r2942710937) inline 2026-03-16 @ test/integration/singlecluster/tas/tas_test.go:590: "`s/limits-pnly/limits-only`"
+  - [PR #13601](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13601#discussion_r4109031609) inline 2026-09-25 @ keps/2941-DRA/README.md:2208: "stray line?"
+  - [PR #8579](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8579#discussion_r2839137471) inline 2026-02-23 @ pkg/controller/jobs/raycluster/raycluster_webhook.go:92: "Stray line, drop this change."
+  - [PR #9232](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9232#discussion_r2806963728) inline 2026-02-14 @ pkg/cache/queue/inadmissible_workloads.go:216: "`s/requeuering/requeuing/g`"
+- **Keep conditions and code readable: break down or name compound conditions (a small named helper), extract a self-contained block into a helper, and move a self-contained part of a very large file into its own file.** Why: about 6 comments in 6 PRs (#8341, #10320, #10785, #17147, #18127, #19172).
+  - [PR #19172](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/19172#discussion_r4647813214) inline 2026-11-20 @ pkg/dra/claims.go:658: "claims.go is past 1,000 lines now."
+  - [PR #18127](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/18127#discussion_r4609150841) inline 2026-11-18 @ pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go:1607: "could this four-line condition be a small named helper"
+  - [PR #17147](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17147#discussion_r4261957794) inline 2026-10-13 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go:594: "Could we break this condition down? It's hard to read with the negated compound at the end"
+
+### Comments and docs
+
+- **[simple] Update every comment, godoc, struct doc and user doc that the change makes false. For user docs, fix or extend the site pages (concepts, tasks, setup) in the same PR, including new limitations of a gate.** Stale comments are the most common comment finding. Why: about 13 comments in 12 PRs (#9322, #10973, #14562, #15049, #15503, #15961, #16060, #16482, #17260, #17526, #17916, #18595).
+  - [PR #15503](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15503#discussion_r4031633019) inline 2026-09-16 @ pkg/util/waitforpodsready/waitforpodsready.go:65: "Comment says "only set when strictly positive" but we set it whenever the pointer is non-nil."

+- **[simple] Keep code and test comments to one or two lines, don't repeat what the case name or the step above already says, and put fix history in the commit message.** Why: about 4 comments in 3 PRs (#12283, #14227, #17269 twice).
+  - [PR #17269](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17269#discussion_r4285746471) inline 2026-10-15 @ test/integration/singlecluster/controller/jobs/trainjob/trainjob_controller_test.go:363: "nit: could we keep this to two lines? The fix history fits better in the commit message."
+  - [PR #14227](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14227#discussion_r4168221835) inline 2026-10-02 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/utils_test.go:51: "most rows carry two-line comments for one-line cases, and the case names already say which key is tested."
+  - [PR #12283](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/12283#discussion_r3420616857) inline 2026-06-16 @ pkg/controller/core/localqueue_controller.go:326: "nit: maybe just one liner stating:"
+- **[simple] Don't put maturity or status words in code comments or KEP criteria.** They go stale. Why: 3 comments in 3 PRs (#11552, #13601, #14130).
+  - [PR #14130](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14130#discussion_r4109131172) inline 2026-09-25 @ pkg/dra/claims.go:185: "nit: drop "the Alpha", it'll go stale at Beta."
+  - [PR #11552](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/11552#discussion_r3292028016) inline 2026-05-23 @ keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1090: "nit: remove `implemented` tags, as graduation criteria describe requirements, not status."
+- **Add a short "why" comment where a behavior change, a skip or a kept-on-purpose check would look like an accident. Where two places must change together (a list mirrored in docs, a switch restated elsewhere, a value with two readers), put a comment at each that names the other.** When the two places can share code instead, they ask for a shared helper "so the two lists can't drift" (#18632, see the reuse rule). Why: about 12 comments in 12 PRs (#8341, #9137, #9232, #13989, #14705, #14784, #15612, #16123, #16779, #17147, #18486, #18595); about 5 of them are "keep in sync" pointers.
+  - [PR #9232](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9232#discussion_r2843258579) inline 2026-02-23 @ pkg/cache/queue/manager.go:326: "Worth a short comment here so it doesn't look like an accidental omission."
+  - [PR #16123](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16123#discussion_r4101402442) inline 2026-09-25 @ pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go:131: "With the fallback, this Inadmissible match has to stay until `UnadmittedWorkloadsObservability` is GA. Worth a comment saying so."
+  - [PR #18595](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/18595#discussion_r4617396112) inline 2026-11-18 @ pkg/controller/admissionchecks/provisioning/provisioning.go:66: "This restates the switch in `syncCheckStates`, so if that changes, the ungater either races the controller again or nobody acts. Can we add a comment there pointing at this function?"
+- **[simple] Leave a `TODO(#issue)` on code that a tracked follow-up will change or remove (a workaround, a walk to be replaced, a setting to flip at Beta).** Why: 3 comments in 3 PRs (#8935, #14705, #18078), plus a KEP variant asking for a removal plan (#17526).
+  <!-- skillsaw-disable-next-line content-placeholder-text -->

+- **[simple] Give a new exported constant, a non-obvious function, and every new API, config or annotation field a doc comment that states its intent, its allowed values, and what special values (0, unset, `None`) mean.** No Go linter in `.golangci.yaml` enforces this, so it reaches review. Why: about 4 comments in 4 PRs (#8963, #11997, #15503, #16535), mostly about undocumented special values.
+  - [PR #8963](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8963#discussion_r2883874468) inline 2026-03-04 @ pkg/scheduler/flavorassigner/tas_flavorassigner.go:208: "nit: Can you add a godoc here explaining the intent?"

Could we document it in both instead?"
+- **[simple] User docs: state the feature gate and alpha status, and follow the page's existing conventions (Note vs Warning, blank lines, wording). Also tell admins what they must do: where the setting has to go, what is already on by default (so nothing is needed), the operational limit it implies, and plainly when not to use it ("do not enable it in production").** The KEP-side "so admins know…" asks fall under the Risks rule. Why: about 8 comments in 4 PRs (#14130, #15049, #16177, #16469); the admin-guidance part is 4 of them in 3 PRs (#14130, #16177 twice, #16469).
+  - [PR #15049](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15049#discussion_r4451471235) inline 2026-11-03 @ site/content/en/docs/concepts/topology_aware_scheduling.md:650: "nit: add a blank line before this heading."
+  - [PR #16469](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16469#discussion_r4160749368) inline 2026-10-01 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/run/dra.md:74: "The other alpha sections here use a Note like"
+  - [PR #16177](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16177#discussion_r4110173640) inline 2026-09-26 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/manage/setup_wait_for_pods_ready.md:23: "without saying the feature is alpha and behind a gate."
+  - [PR #16177](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16177#discussion_r4110165832) inline 2026-09-26 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/manage/setup_wait_for_pods_ready.md:153: "Worth adding for admins: with `blockAdmission: true`, `maxTimeoutOnWorkload` is also the longest a single workload can keep admission blocked for the whole cluster, so set it to what you can tolerate."
+
+### Logging and events
+
+- **[simple] Don't log at V(0) for per-workload or per-cycle events. Follow the level table in `logging_policy.md`: per-object decisions and scheduler cycle progress at V(3), selected decision details at V(4), traces at V(5).** Default logs (`--zap-log-level=2`) must stay quiet. Why: about 8 comments in 5 PRs (#8579, #9808, #10156, #12419 four suggestions, #17649); all 2026-H1. (historical: their V(2) suggestions for per-workload scheduler logs in #12419 predate the logging policy of 2026-10-08, and #17227 then moved scheduler progress logs to V(3); use V(2) only for concise summaries of a material state change.)
+  - [PR #12419](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/12419#discussion_r3474660420) inline 2026-06-25 @ pkg/scheduler/scheduler.go:663: "I think V(0) is too loud."
+  - [PR #9808](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9808#discussion_r2984447414) inline 2026-03-24 @ pkg/scheduler/flavorassigner/flavorassigner.go:215: "Use `log.V(3).Info`"
+- **[simple] Log "should never happen" states (a failed revert, an invalid pool that means a driver bug) where default logs (`--zap-log-level=2`) show them, at the level `logging_policy.md` gives: V(1) for a rare anomaly confined to one controller or the scheduler, V(0) for an invariant violation affecting Kueue as a whole, `Error` only when it disrupts an operation.** Why: 3 comments in 3 PRs (#10366, #15612, #18133). (historical: they asked for V(2) on the invalid-pool log in #18133; the logging alignment #19034, approved by mimowo on 2026-11-19, moved that log to V(1), as the policy says.)
+  - [PR #18133](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/18133#discussion_r4443221672) inline 2026-11-02 @ pkg/dra/pools.go:52: "Could we log the invalid case at V(2) and keep incomplete at V(4), checking `errIncompletePool`?"
+  - [PR #15612](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15612#discussion_r4018359973) inline 2026-09-15 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/snapshot.go:109: "Worth a comment saying so, and you can bump the log level since it shouldn't happen."
+  <!-- skillsaw-disable-next-line content-weak-language -->
+  - [PR #10366](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10366#discussion_r3067988827) inline 2026-04-11 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/tas_non_tas_pod_cache.go:116: "nit: consider adding `log.Error` or `V(0)` instead of `V(2)` so it surfaces in default logs."
+- **[simple] Write status, Events and metrics only on a real change. A repeated reconcile, a repeated scheduling cycle or a restart must not re-emit an Event, count a metric again, re-patch an unchanged object or reset a condition's timestamp.** This fits `logging_policy.md` ("Prefer emitting an Event after a successful write"). It is not the dropped `Eventf`-format rule, which `govet` printf covers. Why: 4 comments in 4 threads (#11720, #14589, #16857, Issue #18371), plus issue analysis #8095.
+  - [PR #16857](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16857#discussion_r4339639770) inline 2026-10-21 @ pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go:262: "Could we emit only when the QuotaReserved reason or message changes"
+  - [PR #14589](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14589#discussion_r4457242487) inline 2026-11-03 @ keps/975-partial-preemption/README.md:525: "Otherwise the event and `kueue_partially_preempted_workloads_total` fire every cycle"
+  - [Issue #18371](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/18371#issuecomment-6889752122) comment 2026-11-15: "Also, only patch when the assignment changes."
+- **Keep a metric's series in step with its object's lifecycle. Delete a series when its object or label goes away, report it again on recreate or resume, and update it on every return path (a requeue, a missing ClusterQueue, a stop policy), so no series is left stale or missing.** This is the opposite failure from the rule above. No contribution guide covers metric series, and it recurs in the custom-metric-labels Beta work they own. When the same gap affects a whole family of series, they accept one follow-up for all of them instead of a fix in one place (#18295). Why: about 6 comments in 5 PRs (#10185, #17413 twice, #18221, #19001, #19267), plus the issue audit #16840 and the deferral in #18295.
+  - [PR #19001](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/19001#discussion_r4606795544) inline 2026-11-17 @ pkg/cache/queue/manager.go:863: "nit: this return also needs `reportLQPendingWorkloads(m, q)`, otherwise a requeue after the ClusterQueue is deleted leaves the metric at 0 while the LocalQueue holds the Workload."
+  - [PR #19267](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/19267#discussion_r4661744205) inline 2026-11-23 @ pkg/controller/core/localqueue_controller.go:518: "With this check, nothing re-reports the AFS usage series on resume, so it stays missing until the next sample (up to `usageSamplingInterval`)."
+  - [PR #18221](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/18221#discussion_r4484209157) inline 2026-11-05 @ pkg/cache/queue/unadmitted_workloads.go:105: "once cq-a is deleted, moving one of its two Workloads to a missing CQ brings cq-a's series back"
+
+### Errors
+
+- **Don't drop errors silently: return them, report them in the field error, or log them as a diagnostic (V(3) at least), and keep "evaluated false" apart from "evaluation failed".** When you log one, follow `logging_policy.md`: no `V(n).Error(...)`, and log an operational error once at the higher control layer. Why: about 7 comments in 6 PRs (#9742 twice, #10320, #10973, #11832, #14131, #17649).
+  - [PR #10320](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10320#discussion_r3263580105) inline 2026-05-19 @ pkg/dra/counters.go:176: "Should we log the error here with V(3) instead of discarding it altogether?"
+  - [PR #14131](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14131#discussion_r3753581465) inline 2026-08-10 @ pkg/controller/core/localqueue_controller.go:226: "I think `usageErr` gets swallowed when `cqInCache` is true."
+  - [PR #9742](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9742#discussion_r2921064734) inline 2026-03-11 @ pkg/dra/claims.go:326: "Errors from DeviceMatches are silently treated as `no match`."
+- **Make invariants hold by construction, not by care: no manual unlocks, no nil used as a hidden flag, no invariant that only a comment keeps.** Why: 3 comments in 3 PRs (#10785, #15961, #17916).
+  - [PR #17916](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17916#discussion_r4403424218) inline 2026-10-28 @ pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go:184: "Passing a nil cache to mean "skip the device check" is easy to hit by accident"
+  - [PR #15961](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15961#discussion_r4097426294) inline 2026-09-24 @ pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go:807: "move the queueing so the invariant holds by construction rather than by comment."
+  - [PR #10785](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10785#discussion_r3224064335) inline 2026-05-12 @ pkg/cache/queue/manager.go:448: "The manual unlock is error-prone if someone adds an early return later"
+
+### Simplicity and reuse
+
+- **[simple] Don't commit dead, unused or unreachable code, guards that can't fire, or checks the apiserver already does.** One exception: they asked to keep a defensive check where a future caller would otherwise silently undercount (#9808, "it's still worth adding as defensive code"). Why: about 14 comments in 12 PRs (#8579, #8963, #9232, #9742, #11832, #14130, #14721, #15503, #16857, #17526, #18127, #18706).
+  - [PR #14130](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14130#discussion_r4109126182) inline 2026-09-25 @ pkg/dra/claims.go:88: "nit: the apiserver already rejects this, fine to drop?"
+  - [PR #14721](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14721#discussion_r3834985164) inline 2026-08-22 @ pkg/controller/jobs/statefulset/statefulset_reconciler.go:194: "nit: This guard can't fire any more."
+  - [PR #8579](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8579#discussion_r2839110040) inline 2026-02-23 @ pkg/controller/jobs/job/ray_utils.go:56: "This is unused dead code. Don't check in dead code. Add it when it's needed"
+- **Search for an existing helper or pattern before writing one, and keep one implementation, not two.** Why: about 15 comments in 13 PRs (#9232, #9755, #11420, #11832, #11997, #12045, #14562, #15612, #16253, #17526, #17916, #18632).
+  - [PR #18632](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/18632#discussion_r4571411927) inline 2026-11-14 @ pkg/config/validation.go:580: "nit: Could we share these name checks with `validateDeviceClassMappings` in a small helper, so the two lists can't drift?"
+  - [PR #15612](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15612#discussion_r4018350441) inline 2026-09-15 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/snapshot.go:84: "I'd keep one, not two."
+  - [PR #11997](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/11997#discussion_r3376470398) inline 2026-06-08 @ pkg/workload/workload.go:1981: "Please use `workloadslicing.IsElasticWorkload` instead."
+  - [PR #11832](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/11832#discussion_r3416156519) inline 2026-06-15 @ pkg/util/tlsconfig/tlsconfig.go:122: "This is redundant as `cliflag.TLSCurvePreferences` already handles empty input and returns nil."
+- **[simple] Don't add a wrapper, exported variant or generic parameter that has one caller. Call the underlying function, or fold the helper into its only caller.** The dead-code rule doesn't cover live but needless indirection. This is about helpers that only forward a call or carry an unused option; a small named helper that makes a condition readable is still welcome (see the readability rule). Why: 3 comments in 3 PRs (#8579, #17119, #17388), plus #14227, where moving the fix into the setters would "drop the exported helpers".
+  - [PR #17388](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17388#discussion_r4460461330) inline 2026-11-03 @ pkg/cache/queue/manager.go:854: "nit: this is the only non-test caller, in the same package, so could we call `cq.requeueIfNotPresent(log, info, true, reason, quotaReservedReason)` and drop `RequeueIfNotPresentImmediately`?"
+  - [PR #17119](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17119#discussion_r4294898887) inline 2026-10-16 @ pkg/controller/admissionchecks/provisioning/provisioning.go:57: "nit: after the trim `isTerminal` only has one caller. Could we fold it into `hasLiveBooking`?"
+  - [PR #8579](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8579#discussion_r2839130989) inline 2026-02-23 @ pkg/controller/jobs/job/ray_utils.go:36: "The `controllerOnly` bool parameter makes this generic but only `isRayRedisCleanupJob` uses it with `controllerOnly=true`."
+- **Put the fix in the one place that covers exactly the paths that need it: a setter every path goes through, or the call sites, rather than a shared function that other callers also use.** Why: about 5 comments in 5 PRs (#10009, #14227, #15961, #17526, #18127).
+  - [PR #14227](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14227#discussion_r4168212912) inline 2026-10-02 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/utils.go:280: "filtering once in those two setters would cover every path and drop the exported helpers and the pod_controller.go change."
+  - [PR #10009](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10009#discussion_r2961407481) inline 2026-03-19 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/defaults.go:51: "Might be better to handle this at the webhook call sites instead of the shared function."
+  - [PR #17526](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17526#discussion_r4408223320) inline 2026-10-29 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/utils.go:267: "Could we keep the gate check here at the call site, as main does, and drop it from `ParseWaitForPodsReady`?"
+
+### API, configuration and feature gates
+
+- **Check both gate states. Gate-off must keep today's behavior, and the gate check belongs in the outer guard. Write it as an explicit `features.Enabled(...)` check even when the data it reads only exists with the gate on, placed where the sibling code or main already has it (inside a shared lookup when sibling lookups check there, at the call site when main does), so the gated path is easy to find.** This is one of their most common correctness findings. Why: about 14 inline comments on non-test code in 11 PRs (#9808, #10320, #11997, #14778, #15503, #16123, #17526, #17991), plus gate-off test requests; the explicit-check part is 3 comments in 3 PRs, all 2026-11 (#18078, #18127, #18632), and the placement part follows #17526 and #18632.
+  - [PR #11997](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/11997#discussion_r3376451022) inline 2026-06-08 @ pkg/scheduler/flavorassigner/tas_flavorassigner.go:64: "This block now runs for elastic workloads even when `ElasticJobsViaWorkloadSlicesWithTAS` is off."
+  - [PR #9808](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9808#discussion_r2984396041) inline 2026-03-24 @ pkg/config/validation.go:554: "but this rejects the config and prevents Kueue from starting."
+  - [PR #18078](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/18078#discussion_r4497349407) inline 2026-11-06 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/clusterqueue.go:572: "nit: could this also check `features.Enabled(features.CustomMetricLabels)`, like the rest of this file, so the gated path is easy to find?"
+- **New startup validation must not crashloop a config that loads today, since a validation error exits the manager. On release branches put the refusal behind an off-by-default gate; on main gate it, warn first, or call it out as ACTION REQUIRED.** Why: about 6 comments in 6 PRs (#9808, #13989, #14227, #14756, #18599, plus their own #19158).
+  - [PR #14756](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14756#issuecomment-5388555585) comment 2026-08-23: "Validation failure calls os.Exit, so the manager crashloops on a 0.18.z or 0.19.z upgrade."
+  - [PR #14227](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14227#issuecomment-5898120091) comment 2026-09-29: "the startup refusal in `labelKeysToCopy` breaks existing configs, so let's follow up with a warning or put it behind a gate."
+  - [PR #13989](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13989#discussion_r3787233398) inline 2026-08-14 @ pkg/config/validation.go:529: "Should this one sit behind `KueueDRAIntegration`?"
+- **Give each Alpha behavior its own gate as a kill switch, even when the feature is opt-in or rides on a Beta gate.** They don't ask for a gate when the change only makes the disabled state fail safe (#10964: "that should be the permanent ungated behavior"). Why: about 5 comments in 5 PRs (#8551, #9251, #13494, #14802, #17804).
+  - [PR #13494](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13494#discussion_r3884123911) inline 2026-08-28 @ keps/13243-multikueue-ray-worker-autoscaling/README.md:317: "Can we either add a dedicated gate or say explicitly why riding a Beta gate is fine here?"
+  - [PR #8551](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8551#discussion_r2839389567) inline 2026-02-23 @ keps/7990-preemption-cost/kep.yaml:1: "I think we need to add a feature gate here."
+  - [PR #9251](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9251#discussion_r2837162818) inline 2026-02-22 @ keps/8902-flavor-aware-drs/README.md:280: "I'd lean toward having a `FlavorAwareFairSharing` gate anyway, for consistency with how Kueue handles other Alpha features"
+- **Before you add new API surface (a new condition, strategy enum value or metric), check whether the existing lifecycle or mechanisms already cover it, since API is hard to remove.** Why: about 4 comments in 4 threads (#8309, #12233, #17433, #18717).
+  - [PR #12233](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/12233#issuecomment-4759995860) comment 2026-06-20: "adds API surface that's hard to remove"
+  - [PR #18717](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/18717#issuecomment-6865368289) comment 2026-11-13: "A max-age alert gets noisy on a busy cluster, and `kueue_unadmitted_workloads` plus the pending resource metric already cover stuck workloads."
+  - [Issue #8309](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/8309#issuecomment-3706270546) comment 2026-01-02: "Adding a new strategy enum to fix this feels like the wrong layer."
+- **Define validation for every new field and annotation: bounds, relations to sibling fields, and what 0 or empty does. When input that is invalid on its own is not rejected at the webhook, say where it is refused and what the user sees.** They test the edge values themselves, for example a 0 that gives a requeue loop with no end. This is not a hard "reject at the webhook" rule: in #13601 they asked why static request shapes were runtime inadmissibility rather than webhook rejections, then agreed to leave the webhook move for Beta (the webhook doesn't read ResourceClaimTemplates, and moving one path alone would create an asymmetry). Why: about 6 comments in 6 PRs (#7370 two suggestion blocks, #13601, #14589, #14700, #15503, #17500).
+  - [PR #14589](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14589#discussion_r4396906475) inline 2026-10-28 @ keps/975-partial-preemption/README.md:252: "What validation do we plan for the new floor?"
+  - [PR #14700](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14700#discussion_r4644541535) inline 2026-11-20 @ keps/5133-capacity-buffer-support/README.md:404: "With `backoffLimitCount` gone, 0 here gives an immediate requeue loop with no end. Minimum 1, and base ≤ max?"
+  - [PR #13601](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13601#discussion_r4047082244) inline 2026-09-18 @ keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1869: "so why are they runtime inadmissibility rather than webhook rejections?"
+- **[simple] Use the right validation mechanism. The Configuration API is not a CRD, so it takes no kubebuilder markers.** Why: 1 explicit statement (#16535); today's `apis/config/v1beta2` has no `kubebuilder:validation` markers, so it matches the code.
+  - [PR #16535](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16535#discussion_r4231293664) inline 2026-10-09 @ keps/16029-pod-local-metrics/README.md:134: "The Configuration API doesn't use kubebuilder validation markers because it isn't a CRD, so this one does nothing."
+
+### Tests
+
+- **Make sure the test fails without the fix. Revert the change, or corrupt the expectation, and run it.** They do this themselves and report the result. Today's `writing_tests.md` asks the same ("For a bug fix, add a case that fails without the fix"). Why: about 22 comments in 21 PRs, almost all from 2026-08 on (#14551, #14585, #14704, #14836, #15001, #16123, #17119, #17294, #17388, #18133, #18595, #18632, #19026, #19172).
```

@@ -0,0 +1,434 @@
+# tenzen-y: review profile
+
+tenzen-y is active in the review data from late 2022 (first entries 2022-11/12) to 2026-11. They act as an approver: 2,932 `/lgtm`, 2,893 `/approve`, and 2,653 entries where both are posted together. They also do release management: 801 `/cherrypick` commands, 516 `/release-note-edit` rewrites (on 446 PRs), 235 `/hold`, and regular "I confirmed the promoted image works well" checks on released images and charts. The review data covers 5,951 threads (4,875 PRs and 1,076 issues; 641 PRs are their own) and 18,479 own entries (7,977 comments, 6,870 inline review comments, 3,632 review bodies). Volume grows every year: 333 entries (2022), 2,544 (2023), 3,913 (2024), 5,376 (2025), 6,313 (2026). Where inline comments land: KEPs 24%, other `pkg/` 14%, scheduler and cache 12%, jobs and jobframework 10%, tests 10%, build/CI/Helm/cmd 9%, API types 7%, docs/site 7%, MultiKueue 3%. The most-reviewed areas are KEP design (TAS, DRA, MultiKueue, concurrent admission, configurable preemption), the scheduler and cache, jobframework and the integrations, the Config and Kueue APIs, Helm, and the release process.
+
+## What they care about most
+
+Shares are of the 11,351 "substantive" own entries, meaning not prow commands only and longer than 25 characters. They come from regex matching, so treat them as rough.
+
+1. **Concrete `suggestion` blocks for small fixes**: 17% of entries (1,934). These are wording in docs and comments, renames, signatures, and test tables.
+2. **API shape and conventions** (fields, `+optional`/`+required` and `omitempty`, validation (strict first, relaxed later), CEL, `Ref` suffix, kind-prefixed type names, API versions, Alpha/Beta semantics): about 10%, the highest share in 2025.
+3. **Justification questions** ("Why do we need…", "Is there any reason…", "Do we need to expose…"): about 6% (691), steady in every year.
+4. **Release notes, cherry-picks (what may be backported) and user-facing impact**: about 10% combined. They rewrite notes themselves (516 notes; about 360 start with an area prefix like `TAS:` or `Helm:`; 26 contain `ACTION REQUIRED`).
+5. **Test structure and coverage** (table-driven tests with maps, comparing errors rather than messages, no test helpers, callbacks or anonymous functions, plain wrappers, adding cases to existing tests, Ginkgo idioms such as `g gomega.Gomega` and `GinkgoHelper`, picking the right level among unit, integration and e2e): about 6%. This theme grew sharply in 2026.
+6. **KEP-first design and scope** (KEP updates, `kep.yaml` milestones and approvers, user stories, stated limitations, yes/no graduation criteria and Alternatives, designs that cover all integrations, one mechanism per user scenario, separate or follow-up PRs without drive-by edits): about 5% KEP/design plus 3% scope. Scope comments doubled in 2026.
+7. **Feature gates and upgrade safety** (guard new Alpha logic, FG state on release branches, keep today's defaults, breaking changes): about 3% FG plus 2% upgrade. In 2026 FG mentions rise to 5%.
+8. **Logging** (verbosity level, propagated logger, structured key/values, `klog.KObj`): about 2%, but the requests are very consistent. Since 2026-10 the repo's Logging Policy settles the levels.
+9. **Go idioms in production code** (no logic in local closures, early return without `else`, named constants instead of literals, nil rather than empty slices, field-named getters, typed values instead of strings, import aliases, compile-time interface assertions, full names for long-lived identifiers, deleting unused code): about 1.5% (about 150 entries). Many are bare `suggestion` blocks, so the share undercounts them.
+
+## Rules for authors
+
+### Naming and style
+
+- **[simple] Don't hold logic in a local closure (`x := func(...) {...}`) inside a function, and don't pass callbacks that mix concerns. In production code, write a named function in the same file. In tests, use wrapper chain methods or `Clone()` of a base object instead of builder closures, callbacks, or `func` fields in test cases (see the inline-objects rule under Tests).** Short predicate literals passed straight to `slices.IndexFunc`/`ContainsFunc`/`DeleteFunc` are fine (they suggest them themselves: #4200, #6203, #15433, 2025–2026), and so is a closure that is the returned value, such as a revert function (#14794). Why: about 35 entries in about 30 PRs, 2025–2026, mostly tests (#5587, #6570, #8431, #8452, #10359, #13573, #14191, #15648, #16398, #18594, #19374); production code in 5 PRs (#4256, #14154, #14918, #15640, #15885). `writing_tests.md` also forbids fixture closures. Evidence:
+  - [PR #15885](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15885#discussion_r4174829380) inline 2026-10-03 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/tas_validation.go:329: "Instead of an anonymous function, we should define a dedicated function in this file."
+  - [PR #8431](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8431#discussion_r2697893654) inline 2026-01-16 @ pkg/cache/queue/manager_test.go:1411: "Those anonymous functions bring us surprises, maintenance PoV in the long-running future."
+  - [PR #14918](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14918#discussion_r3892297106) inline 2026-08-31 @ pkg/controller/tas/node_controller.go:491: "The callback functions can do everything that can be combined into one function, but it's not safe due to anti-separation of concerns."
+- **[simple] Don't put the type in a variable name: `tasSnapshots`, not `tasSnapshotMap`; `wls`, not `workloadList`; `infos`, not `infoSlice` (Google Go style guide). Older (2023) local conventions: `err` prefix for error variables, no `Set` prefix on wrapper setters.** Why: consistency with Go style and the existing code (about 10 typed-name comments in 8 PRs, 2024–2026; the `err`/`Set` conventions were each stated once, in 2023). Evidence:
+  - [PR #3256](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/3256#discussion_r1809784931) inline 2024-10-22 @ pkg/cache/snapshot.go:103: "It would be better to avoid including the typed name in the variable name."
+  - [PR #6760](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6760#discussion_r2368227745) inline 2025-09-22 @ cmd/kueue/main.go:363: "We should not use the `typed` name as part of a variable name. Please see the contributor guide."
+  - [PR #1072](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1072#discussion_r1320347014) inline 2023-09-08 @ cmd/kueue/main.go:72: "We have been adding `err` as a prefix to error variables."
+  - [PR #1072](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1072#discussion_r1324199925) inline 2023-09-13 @ pkg/util/testingjobs/pod/wrappers.go:78: "For consistency, let's keep using setter name without the `Set` prefix."
+- **[simple] Don't export what is used only inside its package. Drop unused parameter names instead of writing `_`.** Why: a smaller API surface ("Do we need to expose this function?" and similar: about 7 entries in 6 PRs, mostly 2023–2024; unneeded `_`: 3 PRs, latest 2026). Evidence:
+  - [PR #2813](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/2813#discussion_r1728529972) inline 2024-08-23 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/noop_webhook.go:30: "Do we need to expose this function?"
+  - [PR #1079](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1079#discussion_r1320303796) inline 2023-09-08 @ pkg/cache/clusterqueue.go:279: "I think we don't need to export to other pkgs."
+  - [PR #13308](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13308#discussion_r4104963704) inline 2026-09-25 @ pkg/controller/jobs/appwrapper/appwrapper_controller.go:108: "No need `_`."
+- **Names must say exactly what the thing covers. Don't call something "generic" when it is specific, and avoid vague words like "common" or a bare "All". Short names are fine only for short-lived locals: exported functions, wide-scope helpers and long-lived struct or API fields get full, descriptive names (`LocalQueueUsage`, not `LqUsage`; `clusterProfileCreds`, not `cpCreds`), and avoid "magic" names like `result`, `change1`.** Why: names shape how users and maintainers understand a feature (about 10 entries in 9 PRs, 2025–2026, mostly API and package names; the scope point in about 7 more PRs, 2024–2025: #2411, #3026, #4200, #4687, #5078, #5426, #7570, mostly bare suggestion renames). Evidence:
+  - [PR #6760](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6760#discussion_r2368175639) inline 2025-09-22 @ pkg/controller/jobs/generic/generic_multikueue_adapter.go:1: "I would like to propose renaming this `externalframeworks` since this is not for generic."
+  - [PR #7419](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/7419#discussion_r2481069053) inline 2025-10-31 @ keps/2724-topology-aware-scheduling/README.md:1007: "I would like to recommend avoiding the `common` prefix since it makes API fields more ambiguous"
+  - [PR #8396](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8396#discussion_r2844368939) inline 2026-02-24 @ keps/7513-quota-check/README.md:135: "Shouldn't we replace this "All" with "AllPodRequests" or "AllPodResources" to explicitly represent that this indicates "Pod"."
+  - [PR #7570](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/7570#discussion_r2543855297) inline 2025-11-19 @ pkg/controller/admissionchecks/multikueue/multikueuecluster.go:354: "Can you use `clusterProfile` here since this is a long-lived struct field and cp is not a well-known shortening."
+- **Don't add near-duplicate helpers with the same meaning. Reuse the existing util or callback (`pkg/util/client`, `pkg/util/slices`, `IntegrationCallbacks`), and put code where the other integrations put it.** Why: maintainability (about 10 entries in 10 PRs, 2025–2026). Evidence:
+  - [PR #6615](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6615#discussion_r2291030399) inline 2025-08-21 @ pkg/workload/workload.go:358: "That will bring us confusion and lack of maintainability."
+  - [PR #13885](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13885#discussion_r3889623198) inline 2026-08-30 @ pkg/scheduler/scheduler_test.go:9548: "We already have util function to collect them."
+  - [PR #13308](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13308#discussion_r4126405421) inline 2026-09-28 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/setup.go:124: "Instead of this one, can you we register quota release strategy to `IntegrationCallbacks`?"
+- **[simple] Use `cmp.Or` for defaulting, the `slices`/`maps` helpers where they apply, and `sets.New[T]()` instead of a hand-made `map[T]struct{}`/`map[T]bool`. Compare with the existing equality helpers: `Quantity.Equal`/`IsZero` (not `Cmp(...) == 0`), `ptr.Equal` (not two `ptr.Deref` calls), `equality.Semantic.DeepEqual` for `ResourceList`s.** Why: shorter idiomatic code (about 30 suggestion blocks in about 22 PRs, plus `sets` in 4 PRs, and the equality helpers in 5 PRs, 2023–2025: #1113, #1897, #2070, #5110, #6570; no enabled linter flags those). Today `golangci-lint` already enforces part of this: `modernize` is on, and `forbidigo` rejects `sort.Slice` and friends (use `slices`) and `ptr.To` (use `new(expr)`, since #18581). Their older `ptr.To` suggestions (2023–2025) are superseded. Evidence:
+  - [PR #13224](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13224#discussion_r3892484276) inline 2026-08-31 @ apis/config/v1beta2/defaults.go:121: "nit: Using cmp.Or capability."
+  - [PR #2195](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/2195#discussion_r1607061346) inline 2024-05-20 @ cmd/kueuectl/app/list/list_workload_printer.go:143: "I would recommend using sets library to avoid maintaining such that map."
+  - [PR #2070](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/2070#discussion_r1589641516) inline 2024-05-03 @ pkg/cache/clusterqueue.go:689: "I'm curious about whether we can use the `Equal` Or `IsZero` function."
+- **[simple] Name getters after the field or value they return (`TotalReserved()`, `RequestableQuota()`), with no `Get` prefix (Effective Go), and don't give a getter-style name to a function that is not a getter. A method that implements an existing interface keeps the interface's name.** Why: 6 PRs, 2023–2026 (#1362, #1385, #5725, #7570, #15000, #15577). In #15577 the author kept `List()` because it implements upstream's `DeviceClassLister`. Evidence:
+  - [PR #15000](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15000#discussion_r4077609453) inline 2026-09-22 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/localqueue.go:59: "NITS: Go uses field names as getter functions."
+  - [PR #5725](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5725#discussion_r2161723436) inline 2025-06-23 @ pkg/cache/tas_flavor_snapshot.go:798: "Go does not recommend implementing getter and setter."
+- **[simple] Follow the import-alias conventions. When a package name collides with a stdlib package imported in the same file, keep the stdlib name and alias the other one: `gocmp` for go-cmp when stdlib `cmp` is also imported (most files without stdlib `cmp` import go-cmp unaliased), `util<name>` for `pkg/util/<name>` (`utilslices`, `utilmaps`, `utilqueue`). Otherwise reuse the alias the package already has elsewhere (`kueue` for the current API version, `metav1`/`corev1`, `clientutil` for `pkg/util/client`), and don't add an alias that isn't needed.** Why: about 10 PRs, 2023–2026 (#849, #1072, #5638, #6676, #6895, #7030, #7570, #9229, #10336, #13885). Today's code: about 280 unaliased go-cmp imports and 8 `gocmp` ones (half of them in files that also import stdlib `cmp`); `pkg/util/client` is `clientutil` 22 times and `utilclient` 6 times, and in #7030 they suggested `clientutil`. revive's `redundant-import-alias` only catches aliases equal to the package name. Evidence:
+  - [PR #6676](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6676#discussion_r2311090499) inline 2025-08-29 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/tas_cache_test.go:20: "We typically keep the std library name."
+  - [PR #9229](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/9229#issuecomment-3902325983) comment 2026-02-14: "we make an alias (`utilXYZ`) if it is the same std package name like `slices`"
+  - [PR #10336](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10336#discussion_r3056421076) inline 2026-04-09 @ pkg/webhooks/clusterqueue_webhook.go:34: "In almost all places, we use `utilqueue`."
+- **[simple] Return early, and drop the `else` after a branch that returns. When two branches differ only in one value, set the value and make one call.** Validation functions are the exception: collect all errors instead of returning on the first one (#9547, 2026). Why: about 8 suggestion edits in 7 PRs, 2024–2025 (#1313, #2331, #3256, #4301, #5256, #6590, #7588); 4 of them say so in words. No enabled linter checks this (revive `superfluous-else`/`indent-error-flow` are off). Evidence:
+  - [PR #3256](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/3256#discussion_r1809822210) inline 2024-10-22 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/tas.go:39: "This `else` is redundant since if requiredFound is true, this function early return the result."
+  - [PR #5256](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5256#discussion_r2091453591) inline 2025-05-15 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/tas_validation.go:39: "nit: This `else` statement does not provide any capability."
+- **[simple] Don't hardcode literal values. Use the existing constants (`corev1.ResourceCPU`, `metav1.NamespaceDefault`, API and `pkg/constants` values), or add a named constant at the top of the file for new delays, paths and keys in production code.** In tests, reuse existing constants, but don't add a new constant for a single case (#14670, #15885; see the inline-objects rule). Why: about 24 entries in 22 PRs, 2023–2026. Evidence:
+  - [PR #11943](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/11943#discussion_r3364001251) inline 2026-06-05 @ pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go:354: "Can you make the delay time a constant at the top of this file?"
+  - [PR #5252](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5252#discussion_r2092270197) inline 2025-05-16 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/integrationmanager.go:216: "Let's use the constants instead of hardcoded value."
+  - [PR #6701](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6701#discussion_r2313801359) inline 2025-09-01 @ pkg/scheduler/scheduler_test.go:3830: "We can leverage the meta API constants."
+- **[simple] Don't initialize empty slices (`[]T{}`, `make([]T, 0)`, `field.ErrorList{}`). Kueue treats nil and empty slices the same, so check them with `len()`.** Why: 12 entries in 11 PRs, 2023–2026. Evidence:
+  - [PR #4380](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/4380#discussion_r1967882823) inline 2025-02-24 @ pkg/controller/jobs/appwrapper/appwrapper_controller.go:129: "There is no need to prepare an empty slice."
+  - [PR #16011](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16011#discussion_r4073718118) inline 2026-09-22 @ pkg/controller/jobs/pod/pod_controller_test.go:225: "We don't distinguish between nil slice and empty slice. So, we always use length comparison to detect a nil slice."
+- **[simple] Use a dedicated named type (a typed reason, `schema.GroupVersionKind`, a typed key) instead of plain strings, and avoid several same-typed string parameters in a row.** Why: 10 entries in 9 PRs, 2024–2026 (#1960, #2376, #4295, #6590, #8786, #14565); the same-typed-parameters point comes from #6590. Evidence:
+  - [PR #6590](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6590#discussion_r2291844495) inline 2025-08-21 @ pkg/workload/workload.go:1084: "is unsafe since we have 3 string (same typed) input"
+  - [PR #8786](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8786#discussion_r2730234342) inline 2026-01-27 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/base_webhook.go:125: "Instead of seaparated stringer "group", "kind" passing, "gvk" is more type safe."
+- **[simple] Assert interface implementations at compile time with `var _ Interface = (*Type)(nil)` next to the type (reconcilers, predicates, runnables, snapshots, `ObjectRefProvider`).** Why: reviewers otherwise can't tell what a struct implements; they call it a "typed contract". Cycle 1 dropped this at 2 PRs; the full suggestion scan finds 8 PRs, 2024–2026 (#1313, #1609, #2102, #2881, #3256, #3266, #6656, #14794), mostly bare suggestion blocks. The Coding Guidelines say the same ("Verify interface compliance at the package level"), and `pkg/` has about 150 such lines. Evidence:
+  - [PR #2102](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/2102#discussion_r1595014821) inline 2024-05-09 @ pkg/util/wait/backoff.go:49: "I'm not really a fan of this style since reviewers often miss if this struct (`speedBackoffManager`) implements any interfaces"
+  - [PR #6656](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6656#discussion_r2299443463) inline 2025-08-26 @ pkg/scheduler/preemption/preemption.go:106: "can you open a PR to implement a compiler-safe interface implementation?"
+  - [PR #14794](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14794#discussion_r3893254535) inline 2026-08-31 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/simulator/default.go:46: "Adding typed contract."
+- **[simple] Delete parameters, return values, fields and wrapper methods that nothing uses, including ones your change made unused. Inline variables and thin helpers that are used once and add no meaning.** Why: unused code in about 8 PRs (#1822, #2331, #4213, #6297, #6570, #6760, #14794), single-use variables and helpers in about 4 PRs (#4606, #5675, #5782, #15824), 2024–2026. The default `unused` linter catches dead functions but not unused return values or parameters. This doesn't conflict with the anonymous-function rule: real logic goes into a named function, trivial one-use wrappers get inlined. Evidence:
+  - [PR #6297](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6297#discussion_r2251148614) inline 2025-08-04 @ pkg/workload/workload.go:1: "The returned value from `ResetChecksOnEviction` has never been used, anywhere."
+  - [PR #6760](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6760#discussion_r2368243176) inline 2025-09-22 @ pkg/controller/jobs/generic/generic_multikueue_adapter.go:108: "The `remoteClient` has never been used."
+  - [PR #5675](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5675#discussion_r2158087461) inline 2025-06-20 @ test/e2e/singlecluster/pytorchjob_test.go:144: "I don't see any value to define the `listOpts` variable since it is used only once."
+- **[simple] Don't add stray blank lines or whitespace-only diffs, and end every file (YAML, OWNERS, go.mod, kep.yaml too) with a newline.** Why: about 8 explicit asks in 7 PRs (#756, #1733, #5059, #8861, #12412, #13224), 2023–2026, plus bare suggestion blocks that delete a blank line, mostly in 2023. revive `empty-lines` only covers the start and end of Go blocks. Evidence:
+  - [PR #13224](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13224#discussion_r3892486262) inline 2026-08-31 @ apis/config/v1beta2/defaults_test.go:135: "can you remove unnecessary empty lines?"
+  - [PR #8861](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8861#discussion_r3047362830) inline 2026-04-07 @ keps/8691-concurrent-admission/kep.yaml:34: "The new line at the end of the file."
+
+### Comments and docs
+
+- **[simple] Remove comments that just restate the code, and don't narrate how the code works: let the code show the mechanism, and comment only on why.** Why: "the code describes it". This is mostly done with empty `suggestion` blocks that delete the comment lines (about 27 such deletions plus about 9 explicit remarks, nearly all from 2026-08 on, in PRs #4228, #13571, #13573, #14485, #14565, #14822). Evidence:
+  - [PR #13571](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13571#discussion_r3792421676) inline 2026-08-16 @ test/integration/singlecluster/controller/core/clusterqueue_controller_test.go:899: "Redundant comment."
+  - [PR #14485](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14485#discussion_r3890018484) inline 2026-08-30 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/tas_validation.go:168: "Such comments should be avoided. We have code."
+  - [PR #13571](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13571#discussion_r3792140261) inline 2026-08-16 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/cache.go:193: "It would be better to avoid leaving comments for HOW."
+- **[simple] Mark temporary code with a `TODO` that names the removal condition and links a tracking issue (`// TODO(#1234): remove once …`), and open the issue if none exists: workarounds for upstream bugs, compatibility shims, test cases kept for deprecated gates, old API versions kept for older Kubernetes, pinned or replaced dependencies. The `// remove in 0.X` comment on a deprecated feature gate (feature-gate entry rule) is the gate-specific case.** Why: otherwise nobody removes the code when the condition is met. 8 PRs, 2023–2026 (#771, #2626, #3043, #4228, #4467, #8575 where they fixed the issue number in the `TODO`, #14705, #18931 where they had a cleanup issue opened for a new `TODO`); 19 of the 36 Go `TODO`s in `pkg/`, `cmd/`, `apis/`, `test/` already use `TODO(#N)`. No guideline or linter asks for it. Evidence:
+  - [PR #4228](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/4228#discussion_r1954769723) inline 2025-02-13 @ pkg/cache/tas_cache_test.go:229: "could you add comments like `TODO: remove after removed LeastAllocatedTAS feature gates` to all removal test cases?"
+  - [PR #3043](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/3043#discussion_r1759281004) inline 2024-09-13 @ config/components/visibility/flowschema.yaml:1: "Additionally, could you open an issue to migrate v1beta3 to v1 once we drop the 1.28 supporting?"
+- **[simple] In user-facing runtime text (log keys and messages, events, conditions, status, metric help), spell names out in full: `TopologyAwareScheduling`, `AdmissionCheck`, `ProvisioningRequest`, `MultiKueue`, `resourceFlavor`, `previous`. Don't write TAS, AC, ProvReq, MK or prev.** Why: about 5 PRs, 2024–2026 (#1313, #6332, #8432, #10264, #17747), partly as bare suggestion blocks. This is not about release notes: the verifier dropped "no abbreviations" there because tenzen-y's own notes use `TAS:`/`AFS:` prefixes. Evidence:
+  - [PR #6332](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6332#discussion_r2247859393) inline 2025-08-01 @ pkg/metrics/metrics.go:168: "We don't say AC, ProvReq, and MK."
+  - [PR #10264](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10264#discussion_r3027430547) inline 2026-04-02 @ pkg/controller/tas/resource_flavor.go:180: "Could you use the full resource name for user facing logging / events / status?"
+  - [PR #17747](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17747#discussion_r4379855637) inline 2026-10-26 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go:851: "Let's use a full word in a user-facing place."
+- **[simple] In docs, KEPs and user-facing messages, write a Kueue or Kubernetes API object as its Kind in PascalCase (`Workload`, `ClusterQueue`, `WorkloadPriorityClass`, `Pod`). Lowercase "workload" means the generic term only. Page titles are the exception: they keep spaced words ("Workload Priority Class", "Resource Flavor").** Why: 4 PRs, 2023–2025 (#1079, #1170, #2698, #5417), many as bare suggestions (`workloadPriorityClass` to `WorkloadPriorityClass` 9 times in #1170). In #1170 they also suggested the title "WorkloadPriorityClass", then accepted trasc's point that titles use spaces like the other pages, which today's concept pages still do. Evidence:
+  - [PR #5417](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5417#discussion_r2126008450) inline 2025-06-04 @ site/content/en/docs/concepts/topology_aware_scheduling.md:108: "In this description, it looks like `workload` indicates `Workload` object instead of generic term."
+  - [PR #2698](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/2698#discussion_r1699480101) inline 2024-08-01 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/run/deployment.md:12: "I think that this `Workload` indicates the Kueue's Workload, right?"
+  - [PR #1079](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1079#discussion_r1322238852) inline 2023-09-12 @ pkg/cache/cache.go:247: "For user-facing error messages, `CluseterQueue` would be more well known."
+- **[simple] Reuse the term Kueue already uses for a concept in KEPs, docs, API names and user-facing text: all-or-nothing scheduling (not "gang"), `within` (as in the ClusterQueue API, not "same"), `effective` (not "usable"), `AdmissionCheck` (not "Checks"), `WorkloadSlice`. Don't add a synonym, use one term per concept throughout a document, and don't stretch an existing term over a different concept.** Why: a new word for a known concept "increases recognition load" for users. About 10 entries in 9 PRs, 2023–2026, 7 of them in 2026 (#537, #8551, #11720, #13308, #13601, #14104, #14310, #14554, #17746). In #14310 they first proposed "all-or-nothing" for an elastic-job scale-up mode, then agreed with the author on "atomic scaling", to keep all-or-nothing for scheduling. The kep-writing skill points the same way ("If a new term clashes with an existing one, qualify it"). Evidence:
+  - [PR #11720](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/11720#discussion_r3404494109) inline 2026-06-12 @ keps/10852-inadmissible-workloads-observability/README.md:295: "The API should be descriptive. The `Checks` is a common word. So, we should use Kueue Kueue-specific `AdmissionCheck` terminology here."
+  - [PR #14554](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14554#discussion_r4025747592) inline 2026-09-16 @ keps/13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md:691: "Can we use "within" instead of "same" since we don't want to use multiple technical terms for the same semantics?"
+  - [PR #14104](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14104#discussion_r3847712213) inline 2026-08-24 @ keps/12382-dynamic-quota-orchestration/README.md:348: "we should avoid using different terminology for the same semantic."
+- **[simple] Write API field docs for end users: refer to fields by their YAML (serialized) names, not Go names, and avoid implementation terms and Go type names.** Why: API docs are user-facing and the reference docs are generated from them (about 8 entries in 8 PRs, 2024–2026). The `commentstart` check in `.golangci-kal.yaml` (`make lint-api`) now enforces only the first word. Evidence:
+  - [PR #4911](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/4911#discussion_r2044506884) inline 2025-04-15 @ apis/kueue/v1beta1/workload_types.go:395: "API documents should use YAML field name since this is user-facing."
+  - [PR #17559](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17559#discussion_r4492181368) inline 2026-11-06 @ keps/13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md:811: "API documentation is user-facing. So, we should use non-implementation-specific terms."
+  - [PR #1960](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1960#discussion_r1559188972) inline 2024-04-10 @ apis/kueue/v1beta1/clusterqueue_types.go:122: "I think that mentioning the type of this field is not valuable for end users."
+- **[simple] In KEPs, describe the design, not the source: don't paste real code, walk through source code step by step, or name variables and functions. The one exception is the API proposal: when the KEP adds or changes an API, it shows the Go types (next rule).** Why: such details go stale and confuse readers (3 PRs, 2025–2026). Evidence:
+  - [PR #7311](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/7311#discussion_r2540543227) inline 2025-11-19 @ keps/6757-failure-recovery/README.md:177: "We strongly recommend not to contain actual code in KEP."
+  - [PR #8396](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8396#discussion_r2850522020) inline 2026-02-25 @ keps/7513-quota-check-strategy/README.md:236: "It would be better to remove implementation details (e.g, variable / typed / function names)."
+  - [PR #13601](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13601#pullrequestreview-5318543857) review 2026-09-25: "Please also avoid writing and explaining step-by-step source code. This is not a textbook for the Kueue mechanism."
+- **[simple] When a KEP adds or changes an API, put the Go types in Design Details: the final Go API surface, with a `json` tag on every field, and only the new or changed fields (leave out existing fields that don't change). A YAML example alone is not enough.** Why: they review field names and tags on the Go types, and unchanged fields hide what the change adds. About 7 PRs, 2025–2026, 5 of them in 2026 (#5981, #6141, #8861, #10877, #11720, #11865, #13163). `cmd/experimental/skills/kep-writing/SKILL.md` rule 13 says the same ("API shows the Go types of the new fields"). Evidence:
+  - [PR #13163](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13163#discussion_r3789138451) inline 2026-08-15 @ keps/6143-quota-release-strategy/README.md:145: "We should also add the Go API proposal after this sentence, which has the final Go API surface."
+  - [PR #8861](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8861#discussion_r3047176211) inline 2026-04-07 @ keps/8691-concurrent-admission/README.md:389: "Additionally, could you add json tags to all fields to review the API carefully?"
+  - [PR #6141](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6141#discussion_r2227232833) inline 2025-07-24 @ keps/4400-workload-priority-preemption-policy/README.md:56: "Could you remove an existing fields from this code snippet, then leave only proposed fields?"
+- **[simple] Attach a minimal YAML manifest to each KEP user story, with only the fields that matter (Kueue Configuration, the job with its annotations, the ClusterQueue).** Why: stated as standing policy in their review of the kep-writing skill, which now says "Add a minimal YAML example to each story" (`cmd/experimental/skills/kep-writing/SKILL.md`); about 8 entries in 6 PRs, all 2026 (#8861, #9225, #13163, #14041, #14310, #17030). Evidence:
+  - [PR #17030](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17030#discussion_r4232466284) inline 2026-10-09 @ cmd/experimental/skills/kep-writing/SKILL.md:41: "I would always request to add YAML example, but it should be minimum example."
+  - [PR #14310](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14310#discussion_r3839343230) inline 2026-08-23 @ keps/12100-partial-scaleup-for-elasticjob/README.md:77: "Can you also attach YAML example in each story?"
+- **Update docs and the website when you add a user-visible feature or feature gate. When a page already covers the topic, extend it instead of adding a dedicated page. When you add, rename or change a user-facing label or annotation, add or update its entry in `site/data/labels_and_annotations.yaml` (`key`, `type`, `example`, `used_on`, `description`), saying what sets it, what it does and which feature gate it requires, and matching the actual behaviour. The Labels and Annotations reference page is generated from that file and sorted by key automatically.** Why: about 45 docs requests in about 40 PRs; they also post `/cherry-pick website` themselves (about 340 times). "Extend instead of a new page" was asked once (2026), and in 2025 they asked for a new dedicated concept page instead of growing an index page, so pick the page by topic. The labels file: nothing generates or checks its entries; asked in 3 PRs, 2024–2025 (#3191, #3649, #5215), then in 2026 they found many entries missing (tracking issue #9256) and, in #19278, suggested lines naming the required feature gate and fixes to behaviour descriptions. Evidence:
+  - [PR #13308](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13308#discussion_r4105052844) inline 2026-09-25 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/manage/setup_quota_release_strategy.md:1: "Instead of a dedicated page, can you just add the description to"
+  - [PR #8530](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8530#issuecomment-3745762774) comment 2026-01-13: "could you cherry-pick this to the website as well? It could deliver the FG specification to online documentation."
+  - [PR #5215](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5215#pullrequestreview-2833320565) review 2025-05-12: "Additionally, could you update the labels reference doc?"
+- **[simple] On a docs page for an Alpha or Beta feature, add a `{{% alert title="Note" %}}` card with the feature gate, its stage and the Kueue version that introduced it. Link to the installation feature-gates section instead of repeating how to enable gates.** Why: about 7 PRs, 2024–2025: version alerts in #1705, #1751, #2053 (2024, then titled "Warning"), the Note card in #6259 and #6270, linking to the installation page in #3898 and #7890; the same ask for KEP sections in #12344 (2026). Today's docs pages use this Note form (about 40 such cards). Evidence:
+  - [PR #6259](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6259#discussion_r2241908574) inline 2025-07-30 @ site/content/en/docs/concepts/admission_fair_sharing.md:30: "Could you mention the Kueue version to enable this enhancement as a `alert title="Note" `?"
+  - [PR #7890](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/7890#discussion_r2564283564) inline 2025-11-26 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/manage/enforce_job_management/opt_in_namespace_management.md:88: "We already mention how to enable feature gate in `Before you begin` section."
+- **Write task pages for their reader (batch user, serving user or admin), as step-by-step usage without internal mechanisms. Put full manifests in `site/static/examples/` and embed them.** Why: about 5 PRs, 2024–2025 (#3432, #3454, #3898, #4283). Evidence:
+  - [PR #3898](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/3898#discussion_r1900493945) inline 2025-01-02 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/run/tektoncd.md:14: "I would like to keep mentioning only end user-facing descriptions here."
+  - [PR #3898](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/3898#discussion_r1900497040) inline 2025-01-02 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/run/tektoncd.md:70: "Could you move Tekton specific manifests to https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/tree/main/site/static/examples?"
+- **[simple] Give every fenced code block in docs and KEPs a language (```` ```yaml ````, ```` ```go ````, ```` ```shell ````).** Why: a small nit in 3 PRs (#1226 2023, #7652 2025, #8861 2026 with 9 suggestions); no markdown linter in the repo checks it. Evidence:
+  <!-- skillsaw-disable-next-line content-tautological -->
+  - [PR #7652](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/7652#discussion_r2525673171) inline 2025-11-14 @ site/content/en/docs/installation/_index.md:240: "The laungauge color could be helpful to understand this code."
+  - [PR #8861](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8861#discussion_r3087662454) inline 2026-04-15 @ keps/8691-concurrent-admission/README.md:200: "It seems that the code block starting marks are missing."
+- **[simple] In new generic examples (docs, KEPs, `values.yaml`, config samples, API comments), use neutral placeholders (`example.com/gpu`, `gpu.example.com`, `example.com/sample`) rather than vendor or product names (`nvidia.com/gpu`, MIG), and don't use `kueue.x-k8s.io/...` keys for labels Kueue doesn't provide (mimowo agreed in #5678).** Why: 3 PRs, 2025–2026 (#5372, #5678, #10283). Not absolute: in #10283 (2026-05) their own suggested DRA counter example used the real `gpu.nvidia.com` driver attributes, and today's docs and KEPs still carry about 260 `nvidia.com/gpu` (against about 90 `example.com/gpu`), so expect the ask on new or touched examples. Evidence:
+  - [PR #5372](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5372#discussion_r2109023735) inline 2025-05-27 @ charts/kueue/values.yaml:132: "Let's make this as vendor-free."
+  - [PR #10283](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10283#discussion_r3268236834) inline 2026-05-19 @ keps/2941-DRA/README.md:1071: "Can you avoid using a vendor-specific name?"
