### Review by [@reruno](https://github.com/reruno) (commented) — 2026-11-25T11:42:40Z

- **cmd/experimental/skills/maintainers-review/SKILL.md:105** by [@reruno](https://github.com/reruno) — 2026-11-25T11:42:40Z
```diff
@@ -0,0 +1,133 @@
+---
+name: maintainers-review
+description: Write and self-review Kueue changes against the review bar of Kueue maintainers, distilled from their real review comments. Use before pushing or opening a PR, when addressing review comments, or when asked to review a PR "like a maintainer".
+license: Apache-2.0
+metadata:
+  copyright: The Kubernetes Authors
+---
+
+# Skill: Maintainers Review
+
+The checklist below holds the requests that Kueue maintainers repeat most often in PR
+reviews. Apply it to your diff before you push, so the reviewer can spend their time on
+design. Each rule, with its reason and linked precedents, is in [rules.md](rules.md).
+
+## How to use
+
+- **Writing code or preparing a PR.** Walk the checklist for your diff. Fix `[simple]`
+  items directly. For the other rules, change the code or explain your choice in the PR
+  description.
+- **Reviewing a PR.** Report a violation with its rule ID (for example `MR-H1`) and the file
+  and line, only where the diff breaks the rule. Cite the rule and its precedent from
+  [rules.md](rules.md) in place of claiming that a named maintainer would object.
+- **Before pushing**, run `make lint-fix`, `make ci-lint`, `make lint-api` and `make verify`,
+  and regenerate generated files (`make generate manifests update-helm`) from their
+  sources. The checklist leaves out what these already check.
+
+## Where the rules come from
+
+They are distilled from the review history of eight current and emeritus Kueue
+approvers (2022 to 2026). Each profile holds more rules and precedents.
+
+| Reviewer | Standing | Profile |
+|---|---|---|
+| mimowo | approver | [reviewers/mimowo.md](reviewers/mimowo.md) |
+| tenzen-y | approver | [reviewers/tenzen-y.md](reviewers/tenzen-y.md) |
+| sohankunkerkar | approver | [reviewers/sohankunkerkar.md](reviewers/sohankunkerkar.md) |
+| mbobrovskyi | approver for releases and kueuectl, reviewer | [reviewers/mbobrovskyi.md](reviewers/mbobrovskyi.md) |
+| alculquicondor | emeritus approver | [reviewers/alculquicondor.md](reviewers/alculquicondor.md) |
+| ahg-g | emeritus approver | [reviewers/ahg-g.md](reviewers/ahg-g.md) |
+| gabesaba | emeritus approver | [reviewers/gabesaba.md](reviewers/gabesaba.md) |
+| kerthcet | emeritus approver | [reviewers/kerthcet.md](reviewers/kerthcet.md) |
+
+## Checklist
+
+### A. Diff and scope
+
+- [ ] `MR-A1` [simple] Change only the lines the fix needs; move code unchanged.
+- [ ] `MR-A2` Send side fixes and refactors as separate PRs; land prep PRs first.
+- [ ] `MR-A3` [simple] Add code with its first user; export only what other packages use.
+- [ ] `MR-A4` Take API changes and new mechanisms through a KEP first.
+
+### B. Simplicity and reuse
+
+- [ ] `MR-B1` [simple] Search for an existing helper, constant or pattern, and reuse it.
+- [ ] `MR-B2` [simple] Delete guards, variables and parameters that change nothing; derive state.
+- [ ] `MR-B3` [simple] Use early returns and named functions, not anonymous functions, for logic.
+- [ ] `MR-B4` [simple] Apply each change to sibling integrations and every similar place.
+- [ ] `MR-B5` [simple] Use named constants, `workload.Reference`, `sets.Set` and typed keys.
+- [ ] `MR-B6` Put feature code in its own file or type; keep generic code generic.
+- [ ] `MR-B7` Write the simpler code first; back performance claims with numbers.
+
+### C. Naming and Go style
+
+- [ ] `MR-C1` [simple] Give names that match the behaviour; rename when it changes.
+- [ ] `MR-C2` [simple] Follow local naming: getters without `Get`, setters without `Set`.
+- [ ] `MR-C3` Use existing Kueue terms and PascalCase kinds; spell names out for users.
+- [ ] `MR-C4` [simple] Use `len()`, `ptr.Deref`, `cmp.Or` and the established import aliases.
+
+### D. Comments and docs
+
+- [ ] `MR-D1` [simple] Write comments that explain why; delete ones that restate code.
+- [ ] `MR-D2` [simple] Update comments, `By` texts and docs that the change made stale.
+- [ ] `MR-D3` [simple] Mark temporary code with a tracking issue and its removal condition.
+- [ ] `MR-D4` Document new fields, enums and exported symbols with user-facing semantics.
+- [ ] `MR-D5` [simple] Update the site docs in the same PR; extend existing pages.
+- [ ] `MR-D6` [simple] Mirror mechanical fixes into zh-CN; leave new prose to its owners.
+
+### E. Logging, errors, events
+
+- [ ] `MR-E1` [simple] Pick log verbosity from the Logging Policy table.
+- [ ] `MR-E2` [simple] Use the propagated logger and `ctx`, with structured key/values.
+- [ ] `MR-E3` Return or log every error once; drop error returns that cannot fail.
+- [ ] `MR-E4` Panic only on internal contract breaks; validate user input.
+- [ ] `MR-E5` [simple] Emit events, conditions and metrics only on change; keep messages short.
+
+### F. Controllers and API calls
+
+- [ ] `MR-F1` Patch only on change, once per object, and select with indexes.
+- [ ] `MR-F2` Clean up in-memory state on every delete path; deep-copy shared objects.
+- [ ] `MR-F3` Handle `Create` after restarts and know the leader-election behaviour.
+- [ ] `MR-F4` Keep event handlers to filtering; retry by returning errors from `Reconcile`.
+- [ ] `MR-F5` Keep webhooks free of API calls, and check UPDATE as well as CREATE.
+
+### G. Feature gates, API, upgrades
+
+- [ ] `MR-G1` Gate new and risky behaviour; check the gate first; keep gate-off unchanged.
+- [ ] `MR-G2` [simple] Alpha off, Beta on; one versioned gate line named by scope.
+- [ ] `MR-G3` Add the smallest API a user story needs; reuse existing fields first.
+- [ ] `MR-G4` Follow Kubernetes API conventions: `Ref`, conditions, status, enum modes.
+- [ ] `MR-G5` Validate new fields strictly with markers or CEL, and test them.
+- [ ] `MR-G6` Keep old objects, configs and defaults working across upgrades.
+
+### H. Tests
+
+- [ ] `MR-H1` Show that each new test and branch fails without the change.
```

  Fixed in 196289d00, but without an exemption. A gate-off test still fails if you delete the new gate check, because the new behaviour then runs with the gate off. So MR-H1 now says that for a gate-off test the line to delete is the gate check, and the SKILL.md checklist item says the same. This keeps every new test able to fail.
  
  _Reply drafted with AI assistance (Claude Code)._

### Review by [@reruno](https://github.com/reruno) (commented) — 2026-11-25T11:42:42Z

- **cmd/experimental/skills/maintainers-review/reviewers/tenzen-y.md:213** by [@reruno](https://github.com/reruno) — 2026-11-25T11:42:42Z
```diff
@@ -0,0 +1,434 @@
+# tenzen-y: review profile
+
+tenzen-y is active in the review data from late 2022 (first entries 2022-11/12) to 2026-11. They act as an approver: 2,932 `/lgtm`, 2,893 `/approve`, and 2,653 entries where both are posted together. They also do release management: 801 `/cherrypick` commands, 516 `/release-note-edit` rewrites (on 446 PRs), 235 `/hold`, and regular "I confirmed the promoted image works well" checks on released images and charts. The review data covers 5,951 threads (4,875 PRs and 1,076 issues; 641 PRs are their own) and 18,479 own entries (7,977 comments, 6,870 inline review comments, 3,632 review bodies). Volume grows every year: 333 entries (2022), 2,544 (2023), 3,913 (2024), 5,376 (2025), 6,313 (2026). Where inline comments land: KEPs 24%, other `pkg/` 14%, scheduler and cache 12%, jobs and jobframework 10%, tests 10%, build/CI/Helm/cmd 9%, API types 7%, docs/site 7%, MultiKueue 3%. The most-reviewed areas are KEP design (TAS, DRA, MultiKueue, concurrent admission, configurable preemption), the scheduler and cache, jobframework and the integrations, the Config and Kueue APIs, Helm, and the release process.
+
+## What they care about most
+
+Shares are of the 11,351 "substantive" own entries, meaning not prow commands only and longer than 25 characters. They come from regex matching, so treat them as rough.
+
+1. **Concrete `suggestion` blocks for small fixes**: 17% of entries (1,934). These are wording in docs and comments, renames, signatures, and test tables.
+2. **API shape and conventions** (fields, `+optional`/`+required` and `omitempty`, validation (strict first, relaxed later), CEL, `Ref` suffix, kind-prefixed type names, API versions, Alpha/Beta semantics): about 10%, the highest share in 2025.
+3. **Justification questions** ("Why do we need…", "Is there any reason…", "Do we need to expose…"): about 6% (691), steady in every year.
+4. **Release notes, cherry-picks (what may be backported) and user-facing impact**: about 10% combined. They rewrite notes themselves (516 notes; about 360 start with an area prefix like `TAS:` or `Helm:`; 26 contain `ACTION REQUIRED`).
+5. **Test structure and coverage** (table-driven tests with maps, comparing errors rather than messages, no test helpers, callbacks or anonymous functions, plain wrappers, adding cases to existing tests, Ginkgo idioms such as `g gomega.Gomega` and `GinkgoHelper`, picking the right level among unit, integration and e2e): about 6%. This theme grew sharply in 2026.
+6. **KEP-first design and scope** (KEP updates, `kep.yaml` milestones and approvers, user stories, stated limitations, yes/no graduation criteria and Alternatives, designs that cover all integrations, one mechanism per user scenario, separate or follow-up PRs without drive-by edits): about 5% KEP/design plus 3% scope. Scope comments doubled in 2026.
+7. **Feature gates and upgrade safety** (guard new Alpha logic, FG state on release branches, keep today's defaults, breaking changes): about 3% FG plus 2% upgrade. In 2026 FG mentions rise to 5%.
+8. **Logging** (verbosity level, propagated logger, structured key/values, `klog.KObj`): about 2%, but the requests are very consistent. Since 2026-10 the repo's Logging Policy settles the levels.
+9. **Go idioms in production code** (no logic in local closures, early return without `else`, named constants instead of literals, nil rather than empty slices, field-named getters, typed values instead of strings, import aliases, compile-time interface assertions, full names for long-lived identifiers, deleting unused code): about 1.5% (about 150 entries). Many are bare `suggestion` blocks, so the share undercounts them.
+
+## Rules for authors
+
+### Naming and style
+
+- **[simple] Don't hold logic in a local closure (`x := func(...) {...}`) inside a function, and don't pass callbacks that mix concerns. In production code, write a named function in the same file. In tests, use wrapper chain methods or `Clone()` of a base object instead of builder closures, callbacks, or `func` fields in test cases (see the inline-objects rule under Tests).** Short predicate literals passed straight to `slices.IndexFunc`/`ContainsFunc`/`DeleteFunc` are fine (they suggest them themselves: #4200, #6203, #15433, 2025–2026), and so is a closure that is the returned value, such as a revert function (#14794). Why: about 35 entries in about 30 PRs, 2025–2026, mostly tests (#5587, #6570, #8431, #8452, #10359, #13573, #14191, #15648, #16398, #18594, #19374); production code in 5 PRs (#4256, #14154, #14918, #15640, #15885). `writing_tests.md` also forbids fixture closures. Evidence:
+  - [PR #15885](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15885#discussion_r4174829380) inline 2026-10-03 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/tas_validation.go:329: "Instead of an anonymous function, we should define a dedicated function in this file."
+  - [PR #8431](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8431#discussion_r2697893654) inline 2026-01-16 @ pkg/cache/queue/manager_test.go:1411: "Those anonymous functions bring us surprises, maintenance PoV in the long-running future."
+  - [PR #14918](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14918#discussion_r3892297106) inline 2026-08-31 @ pkg/controller/tas/node_controller.go:491: "The callback functions can do everything that can be combined into one function, but it's not safe due to anti-separation of concerns."
+- **[simple] Don't put the type in a variable name: `tasSnapshots`, not `tasSnapshotMap`; `wls`, not `workloadList`; `infos`, not `infoSlice` (Google Go style guide). Older (2023) local conventions: `err` prefix for error variables, no `Set` prefix on wrapper setters.** Why: consistency with Go style and the existing code (about 10 typed-name comments in 8 PRs, 2024–2026; the `err`/`Set` conventions were each stated once, in 2023). Evidence:
+  - [PR #3256](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/3256#discussion_r1809784931) inline 2024-10-22 @ pkg/cache/snapshot.go:103: "It would be better to avoid including the typed name in the variable name."
+  - [PR #6760](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6760#discussion_r2368227745) inline 2025-09-22 @ cmd/kueue/main.go:363: "We should not use the `typed` name as part of a variable name. Please see the contributor guide."
+  - [PR #1072](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1072#discussion_r1320347014) inline 2023-09-08 @ cmd/kueue/main.go:72: "We have been adding `err` as a prefix to error variables."
+  - [PR #1072](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1072#discussion_r1324199925) inline 2023-09-13 @ pkg/util/testingjobs/pod/wrappers.go:78: "For consistency, let's keep using setter name without the `Set` prefix."
+- **[simple] Don't export what is used only inside its package. Drop unused parameter names instead of writing `_`.** Why: a smaller API surface ("Do we need to expose this function?" and similar: about 7 entries in 6 PRs, mostly 2023–2024; unneeded `_`: 3 PRs, latest 2026). Evidence:
+  - [PR #2813](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/2813#discussion_r1728529972) inline 2024-08-23 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/noop_webhook.go:30: "Do we need to expose this function?"
+  - [PR #1079](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1079#discussion_r1320303796) inline 2023-09-08 @ pkg/cache/clusterqueue.go:279: "I think we don't need to export to other pkgs."
+  - [PR #13308](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13308#discussion_r4104963704) inline 2026-09-25 @ pkg/controller/jobs/appwrapper/appwrapper_controller.go:108: "No need `_`."
+- **Names must say exactly what the thing covers. Don't call something "generic" when it is specific, and avoid vague words like "common" or a bare "All". Short names are fine only for short-lived locals: exported functions, wide-scope helpers and long-lived struct or API fields get full, descriptive names (`LocalQueueUsage`, not `LqUsage`; `clusterProfileCreds`, not `cpCreds`), and avoid "magic" names like `result`, `change1`.** Why: names shape how users and maintainers understand a feature (about 10 entries in 9 PRs, 2025–2026, mostly API and package names; the scope point in about 7 more PRs, 2024–2025: #2411, #3026, #4200, #4687, #5078, #5426, #7570, mostly bare suggestion renames). Evidence:
+  - [PR #6760](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6760#discussion_r2368175639) inline 2025-09-22 @ pkg/controller/jobs/generic/generic_multikueue_adapter.go:1: "I would like to propose renaming this `externalframeworks` since this is not for generic."
+  - [PR #7419](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/7419#discussion_r2481069053) inline 2025-10-31 @ keps/2724-topology-aware-scheduling/README.md:1007: "I would like to recommend avoiding the `common` prefix since it makes API fields more ambiguous"
+  - [PR #8396](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8396#discussion_r2844368939) inline 2026-02-24 @ keps/7513-quota-check/README.md:135: "Shouldn't we replace this "All" with "AllPodRequests" or "AllPodResources" to explicitly represent that this indicates "Pod"."
+  - [PR #7570](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/7570#discussion_r2543855297) inline 2025-11-19 @ pkg/controller/admissionchecks/multikueue/multikueuecluster.go:354: "Can you use `clusterProfile` here since this is a long-lived struct field and cp is not a well-known shortening."
+- **Don't add near-duplicate helpers with the same meaning. Reuse the existing util or callback (`pkg/util/client`, `pkg/util/slices`, `IntegrationCallbacks`), and put code where the other integrations put it.** Why: maintainability (about 10 entries in 10 PRs, 2025–2026). Evidence:
+  - [PR #6615](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6615#discussion_r2291030399) inline 2025-08-21 @ pkg/workload/workload.go:358: "That will bring us confusion and lack of maintainability."
+  - [PR #13885](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13885#discussion_r3889623198) inline 2026-08-30 @ pkg/scheduler/scheduler_test.go:9548: "We already have util function to collect them."
+  - [PR #13308](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13308#discussion_r4126405421) inline 2026-09-28 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/setup.go:124: "Instead of this one, can you we register quota release strategy to `IntegrationCallbacks`?"
+- **[simple] Use `cmp.Or` for defaulting, the `slices`/`maps` helpers where they apply, and `sets.New[T]()` instead of a hand-made `map[T]struct{}`/`map[T]bool`. Compare with the existing equality helpers: `Quantity.Equal`/`IsZero` (not `Cmp(...) == 0`), `ptr.Equal` (not two `ptr.Deref` calls), `equality.Semantic.DeepEqual` for `ResourceList`s.** Why: shorter idiomatic code (about 30 suggestion blocks in about 22 PRs, plus `sets` in 4 PRs, and the equality helpers in 5 PRs, 2023–2025: #1113, #1897, #2070, #5110, #6570; no enabled linter flags those). Today `golangci-lint` already enforces part of this: `modernize` is on, and `forbidigo` rejects `sort.Slice` and friends (use `slices`) and `ptr.To` (use `new(expr)`, since #18581). Their older `ptr.To` suggestions (2023–2025) are superseded. Evidence:
+  - [PR #13224](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13224#discussion_r3892484276) inline 2026-08-31 @ apis/config/v1beta2/defaults.go:121: "nit: Using cmp.Or capability."
+  - [PR #2195](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/2195#discussion_r1607061346) inline 2024-05-20 @ cmd/kueuectl/app/list/list_workload_printer.go:143: "I would recommend using sets library to avoid maintaining such that map."
+  - [PR #2070](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/2070#discussion_r1589641516) inline 2024-05-03 @ pkg/cache/clusterqueue.go:689: "I'm curious about whether we can use the `Equal` Or `IsZero` function."
+- **[simple] Name getters after the field or value they return (`TotalReserved()`, `RequestableQuota()`), with no `Get` prefix (Effective Go), and don't give a getter-style name to a function that is not a getter. A method that implements an existing interface keeps the interface's name.** Why: 6 PRs, 2023–2026 (#1362, #1385, #5725, #7570, #15000, #15577). In #15577 the author kept `List()` because it implements upstream's `DeviceClassLister`. Evidence:
+  - [PR #15000](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15000#discussion_r4077609453) inline 2026-09-22 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/localqueue.go:59: "NITS: Go uses field names as getter functions."
+  - [PR #5725](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5725#discussion_r2161723436) inline 2025-06-23 @ pkg/cache/tas_flavor_snapshot.go:798: "Go does not recommend implementing getter and setter."
+- **[simple] Follow the import-alias conventions. When a package name collides with a stdlib package imported in the same file, keep the stdlib name and alias the other one: `gocmp` for go-cmp when stdlib `cmp` is also imported (most files without stdlib `cmp` import go-cmp unaliased), `util<name>` for `pkg/util/<name>` (`utilslices`, `utilmaps`, `utilqueue`). Otherwise reuse the alias the package already has elsewhere (`kueue` for the current API version, `metav1`/`corev1`, `clientutil` for `pkg/util/client`), and don't add an alias that isn't needed.** Why: about 10 PRs, 2023–2026 (#849, #1072, #5638, #6676, #6895, #7030, #7570, #9229, #10336, #13885). Today's code: about 280 unaliased go-cmp imports and 8 `gocmp` ones (half of them in files that also import stdlib `cmp`); `pkg/util/client` is `clientutil` 22 times and `utilclient` 6 times, and in #7030 they suggested `clientutil`. revive's `redundant-import-alias` only catches aliases equal to the package name. Evidence:
+  - [PR #6676](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6676#discussion_r2311090499) inline 2025-08-29 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/tas_cache_test.go:20: "We typically keep the std library name."
+  - [PR #9229](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/9229#issuecomment-3902325983) comment 2026-02-14: "we make an alias (`utilXYZ`) if it is the same std package name like `slices`"
+  - [PR #10336](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10336#discussion_r3056421076) inline 2026-04-09 @ pkg/webhooks/clusterqueue_webhook.go:34: "In almost all places, we use `utilqueue`."
+- **[simple] Return early, and drop the `else` after a branch that returns. When two branches differ only in one value, set the value and make one call.** Validation functions are the exception: collect all errors instead of returning on the first one (#9547, 2026). Why: about 8 suggestion edits in 7 PRs, 2024–2025 (#1313, #2331, #3256, #4301, #5256, #6590, #7588); 4 of them say so in words. No enabled linter checks this (revive `superfluous-else`/`indent-error-flow` are off). Evidence:
+  - [PR #3256](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/3256#discussion_r1809822210) inline 2024-10-22 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/tas.go:39: "This `else` is redundant since if requiredFound is true, this function early return the result."
+  - [PR #5256](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5256#discussion_r2091453591) inline 2025-05-15 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/tas_validation.go:39: "nit: This `else` statement does not provide any capability."
+- **[simple] Don't hardcode literal values. Use the existing constants (`corev1.ResourceCPU`, `metav1.NamespaceDefault`, API and `pkg/constants` values), or add a named constant at the top of the file for new delays, paths and keys in production code.** In tests, reuse existing constants, but don't add a new constant for a single case (#14670, #15885; see the inline-objects rule). Why: about 24 entries in 22 PRs, 2023–2026. Evidence:
+  - [PR #11943](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/11943#discussion_r3364001251) inline 2026-06-05 @ pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go:354: "Can you make the delay time a constant at the top of this file?"
+  - [PR #5252](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5252#discussion_r2092270197) inline 2025-05-16 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/integrationmanager.go:216: "Let's use the constants instead of hardcoded value."
+  - [PR #6701](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6701#discussion_r2313801359) inline 2025-09-01 @ pkg/scheduler/scheduler_test.go:3830: "We can leverage the meta API constants."
+- **[simple] Don't initialize empty slices (`[]T{}`, `make([]T, 0)`, `field.ErrorList{}`). Kueue treats nil and empty slices the same, so check them with `len()`.** Why: 12 entries in 11 PRs, 2023–2026. Evidence:
+  - [PR #4380](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/4380#discussion_r1967882823) inline 2025-02-24 @ pkg/controller/jobs/appwrapper/appwrapper_controller.go:129: "There is no need to prepare an empty slice."
+  - [PR #16011](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16011#discussion_r4073718118) inline 2026-09-22 @ pkg/controller/jobs/pod/pod_controller_test.go:225: "We don't distinguish between nil slice and empty slice. So, we always use length comparison to detect a nil slice."
+- **[simple] Use a dedicated named type (a typed reason, `schema.GroupVersionKind`, a typed key) instead of plain strings, and avoid several same-typed string parameters in a row.** Why: 10 entries in 9 PRs, 2024–2026 (#1960, #2376, #4295, #6590, #8786, #14565); the same-typed-parameters point comes from #6590. Evidence:
+  - [PR #6590](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6590#discussion_r2291844495) inline 2025-08-21 @ pkg/workload/workload.go:1084: "is unsafe since we have 3 string (same typed) input"
+  - [PR #8786](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8786#discussion_r2730234342) inline 2026-01-27 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/base_webhook.go:125: "Instead of seaparated stringer "group", "kind" passing, "gvk" is more type safe."
+- **[simple] Assert interface implementations at compile time with `var _ Interface = (*Type)(nil)` next to the type (reconcilers, predicates, runnables, snapshots, `ObjectRefProvider`).** Why: reviewers otherwise can't tell what a struct implements; they call it a "typed contract". Cycle 1 dropped this at 2 PRs; the full suggestion scan finds 8 PRs, 2024–2026 (#1313, #1609, #2102, #2881, #3256, #3266, #6656, #14794), mostly bare suggestion blocks. The Coding Guidelines say the same ("Verify interface compliance at the package level"), and `pkg/` has about 150 such lines. Evidence:
+  - [PR #2102](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/2102#discussion_r1595014821) inline 2024-05-09 @ pkg/util/wait/backoff.go:49: "I'm not really a fan of this style since reviewers often miss if this struct (`speedBackoffManager`) implements any interfaces"
+  - [PR #6656](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6656#discussion_r2299443463) inline 2025-08-26 @ pkg/scheduler/preemption/preemption.go:106: "can you open a PR to implement a compiler-safe interface implementation?"
+  - [PR #14794](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14794#discussion_r3893254535) inline 2026-08-31 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/simulator/default.go:46: "Adding typed contract."
+- **[simple] Delete parameters, return values, fields and wrapper methods that nothing uses, including ones your change made unused. Inline variables and thin helpers that are used once and add no meaning.** Why: unused code in about 8 PRs (#1822, #2331, #4213, #6297, #6570, #6760, #14794), single-use variables and helpers in about 4 PRs (#4606, #5675, #5782, #15824), 2024–2026. The default `unused` linter catches dead functions but not unused return values or parameters. This doesn't conflict with the anonymous-function rule: real logic goes into a named function, trivial one-use wrappers get inlined. Evidence:
+  - [PR #6297](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6297#discussion_r2251148614) inline 2025-08-04 @ pkg/workload/workload.go:1: "The returned value from `ResetChecksOnEviction` has never been used, anywhere."
+  - [PR #6760](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6760#discussion_r2368243176) inline 2025-09-22 @ pkg/controller/jobs/generic/generic_multikueue_adapter.go:108: "The `remoteClient` has never been used."
+  - [PR #5675](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5675#discussion_r2158087461) inline 2025-06-20 @ test/e2e/singlecluster/pytorchjob_test.go:144: "I don't see any value to define the `listOpts` variable since it is used only once."
+- **[simple] Don't add stray blank lines or whitespace-only diffs, and end every file (YAML, OWNERS, go.mod, kep.yaml too) with a newline.** Why: about 8 explicit asks in 7 PRs (#756, #1733, #5059, #8861, #12412, #13224), 2023–2026, plus bare suggestion blocks that delete a blank line, mostly in 2023. revive `empty-lines` only covers the start and end of Go blocks. Evidence:
+  - [PR #13224](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13224#discussion_r3892486262) inline 2026-08-31 @ apis/config/v1beta2/defaults_test.go:135: "can you remove unnecessary empty lines?"
+  - [PR #8861](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8861#discussion_r3047362830) inline 2026-04-07 @ keps/8691-concurrent-admission/kep.yaml:34: "The new line at the end of the file."
+
+### Comments and docs
+
+- **[simple] Remove comments that just restate the code, and don't narrate how the code works: let the code show the mechanism, and comment only on why.** Why: "the code describes it". This is mostly done with empty `suggestion` blocks that delete the comment lines (about 27 such deletions plus about 9 explicit remarks, nearly all from 2026-08 on, in PRs #4228, #13571, #13573, #14485, #14565, #14822). Evidence:
+  - [PR #13571](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13571#discussion_r3792421676) inline 2026-08-16 @ test/integration/singlecluster/controller/core/clusterqueue_controller_test.go:899: "Redundant comment."
+  - [PR #14485](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14485#discussion_r3890018484) inline 2026-08-30 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/tas_validation.go:168: "Such comments should be avoided. We have code."
+  - [PR #13571](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13571#discussion_r3792140261) inline 2026-08-16 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/cache.go:193: "It would be better to avoid leaving comments for HOW."
+- **[simple] Mark temporary code with a `TODO` that names the removal condition and links a tracking issue (`// TODO(#1234): remove once …`), and open the issue if none exists: workarounds for upstream bugs, compatibility shims, test cases kept for deprecated gates, old API versions kept for older Kubernetes, pinned or replaced dependencies. The `// remove in 0.X` comment on a deprecated feature gate (feature-gate entry rule) is the gate-specific case.** Why: otherwise nobody removes the code when the condition is met. 8 PRs, 2023–2026 (#771, #2626, #3043, #4228, #4467, #8575 where they fixed the issue number in the `TODO`, #14705, #18931 where they had a cleanup issue opened for a new `TODO`); 19 of the 36 Go `TODO`s in `pkg/`, `cmd/`, `apis/`, `test/` already use `TODO(#N)`. No guideline or linter asks for it. Evidence:
+  - [PR #4228](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/4228#discussion_r1954769723) inline 2025-02-13 @ pkg/cache/tas_cache_test.go:229: "could you add comments like `TODO: remove after removed LeastAllocatedTAS feature gates` to all removal test cases?"
+  - [PR #3043](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/3043#discussion_r1759281004) inline 2024-09-13 @ config/components/visibility/flowschema.yaml:1: "Additionally, could you open an issue to migrate v1beta3 to v1 once we drop the 1.28 supporting?"
+- **[simple] In user-facing runtime text (log keys and messages, events, conditions, status, metric help), spell names out in full: `TopologyAwareScheduling`, `AdmissionCheck`, `ProvisioningRequest`, `MultiKueue`, `resourceFlavor`, `previous`. Don't write TAS, AC, ProvReq, MK or prev.** Why: about 5 PRs, 2024–2026 (#1313, #6332, #8432, #10264, #17747), partly as bare suggestion blocks. This is not about release notes: the verifier dropped "no abbreviations" there because tenzen-y's own notes use `TAS:`/`AFS:` prefixes. Evidence:
+  - [PR #6332](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6332#discussion_r2247859393) inline 2025-08-01 @ pkg/metrics/metrics.go:168: "We don't say AC, ProvReq, and MK."
+  - [PR #10264](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10264#discussion_r3027430547) inline 2026-04-02 @ pkg/controller/tas/resource_flavor.go:180: "Could you use the full resource name for user facing logging / events / status?"
+  - [PR #17747](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17747#discussion_r4379855637) inline 2026-10-26 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go:851: "Let's use a full word in a user-facing place."
+- **[simple] In docs, KEPs and user-facing messages, write a Kueue or Kubernetes API object as its Kind in PascalCase (`Workload`, `ClusterQueue`, `WorkloadPriorityClass`, `Pod`). Lowercase "workload" means the generic term only. Page titles are the exception: they keep spaced words ("Workload Priority Class", "Resource Flavor").** Why: 4 PRs, 2023–2025 (#1079, #1170, #2698, #5417), many as bare suggestions (`workloadPriorityClass` to `WorkloadPriorityClass` 9 times in #1170). In #1170 they also suggested the title "WorkloadPriorityClass", then accepted trasc's point that titles use spaces like the other pages, which today's concept pages still do. Evidence:
+  - [PR #5417](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5417#discussion_r2126008450) inline 2025-06-04 @ site/content/en/docs/concepts/topology_aware_scheduling.md:108: "In this description, it looks like `workload` indicates `Workload` object instead of generic term."
+  - [PR #2698](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/2698#discussion_r1699480101) inline 2024-08-01 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/run/deployment.md:12: "I think that this `Workload` indicates the Kueue's Workload, right?"
+  - [PR #1079](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1079#discussion_r1322238852) inline 2023-09-12 @ pkg/cache/cache.go:247: "For user-facing error messages, `CluseterQueue` would be more well known."
+- **[simple] Reuse the term Kueue already uses for a concept in KEPs, docs, API names and user-facing text: all-or-nothing scheduling (not "gang"), `within` (as in the ClusterQueue API, not "same"), `effective` (not "usable"), `AdmissionCheck` (not "Checks"), `WorkloadSlice`. Don't add a synonym, use one term per concept throughout a document, and don't stretch an existing term over a different concept.** Why: a new word for a known concept "increases recognition load" for users. About 10 entries in 9 PRs, 2023–2026, 7 of them in 2026 (#537, #8551, #11720, #13308, #13601, #14104, #14310, #14554, #17746). In #14310 they first proposed "all-or-nothing" for an elastic-job scale-up mode, then agreed with the author on "atomic scaling", to keep all-or-nothing for scheduling. The kep-writing skill points the same way ("If a new term clashes with an existing one, qualify it"). Evidence:
+  - [PR #11720](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/11720#discussion_r3404494109) inline 2026-06-12 @ keps/10852-inadmissible-workloads-observability/README.md:295: "The API should be descriptive. The `Checks` is a common word. So, we should use Kueue Kueue-specific `AdmissionCheck` terminology here."
+  - [PR #14554](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14554#discussion_r4025747592) inline 2026-09-16 @ keps/13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md:691: "Can we use "within" instead of "same" since we don't want to use multiple technical terms for the same semantics?"
+  - [PR #14104](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14104#discussion_r3847712213) inline 2026-08-24 @ keps/12382-dynamic-quota-orchestration/README.md:348: "we should avoid using different terminology for the same semantic."
+- **[simple] Write API field docs for end users: refer to fields by their YAML (serialized) names, not Go names, and avoid implementation terms and Go type names.** Why: API docs are user-facing and the reference docs are generated from them (about 8 entries in 8 PRs, 2024–2026). The `commentstart` check in `.golangci-kal.yaml` (`make lint-api`) now enforces only the first word. Evidence:
+  - [PR #4911](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/4911#discussion_r2044506884) inline 2025-04-15 @ apis/kueue/v1beta1/workload_types.go:395: "API documents should use YAML field name since this is user-facing."
+  - [PR #17559](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17559#discussion_r4492181368) inline 2026-11-06 @ keps/13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md:811: "API documentation is user-facing. So, we should use non-implementation-specific terms."
+  - [PR #1960](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1960#discussion_r1559188972) inline 2024-04-10 @ apis/kueue/v1beta1/clusterqueue_types.go:122: "I think that mentioning the type of this field is not valuable for end users."
+- **[simple] In KEPs, describe the design, not the source: don't paste real code, walk through source code step by step, or name variables and functions. The one exception is the API proposal: when the KEP adds or changes an API, it shows the Go types (next rule).** Why: such details go stale and confuse readers (3 PRs, 2025–2026). Evidence:
+  - [PR #7311](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/7311#discussion_r2540543227) inline 2025-11-19 @ keps/6757-failure-recovery/README.md:177: "We strongly recommend not to contain actual code in KEP."
+  - [PR #8396](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8396#discussion_r2850522020) inline 2026-02-25 @ keps/7513-quota-check-strategy/README.md:236: "It would be better to remove implementation details (e.g, variable / typed / function names)."
+  - [PR #13601](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13601#pullrequestreview-5318543857) review 2026-09-25: "Please also avoid writing and explaining step-by-step source code. This is not a textbook for the Kueue mechanism."
+- **[simple] When a KEP adds or changes an API, put the Go types in Design Details: the final Go API surface, with a `json` tag on every field, and only the new or changed fields (leave out existing fields that don't change). A YAML example alone is not enough.** Why: they review field names and tags on the Go types, and unchanged fields hide what the change adds. About 7 PRs, 2025–2026, 5 of them in 2026 (#5981, #6141, #8861, #10877, #11720, #11865, #13163). `cmd/experimental/skills/kep-writing/SKILL.md` rule 13 says the same ("API shows the Go types of the new fields"). Evidence:
+  - [PR #13163](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13163#discussion_r3789138451) inline 2026-08-15 @ keps/6143-quota-release-strategy/README.md:145: "We should also add the Go API proposal after this sentence, which has the final Go API surface."
+  - [PR #8861](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8861#discussion_r3047176211) inline 2026-04-07 @ keps/8691-concurrent-admission/README.md:389: "Additionally, could you add json tags to all fields to review the API carefully?"
+  - [PR #6141](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6141#discussion_r2227232833) inline 2025-07-24 @ keps/4400-workload-priority-preemption-policy/README.md:56: "Could you remove an existing fields from this code snippet, then leave only proposed fields?"
+- **[simple] Attach a minimal YAML manifest to each KEP user story, with only the fields that matter (Kueue Configuration, the job with its annotations, the ClusterQueue).** Why: stated as standing policy in their review of the kep-writing skill, which now says "Add a minimal YAML example to each story" (`cmd/experimental/skills/kep-writing/SKILL.md`); about 8 entries in 6 PRs, all 2026 (#8861, #9225, #13163, #14041, #14310, #17030). Evidence:
+  - [PR #17030](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17030#discussion_r4232466284) inline 2026-10-09 @ cmd/experimental/skills/kep-writing/SKILL.md:41: "I would always request to add YAML example, but it should be minimum example."
+  - [PR #14310](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14310#discussion_r3839343230) inline 2026-08-23 @ keps/12100-partial-scaleup-for-elasticjob/README.md:77: "Can you also attach YAML example in each story?"
+- **Update docs and the website when you add a user-visible feature or feature gate. When a page already covers the topic, extend it instead of adding a dedicated page. When you add, rename or change a user-facing label or annotation, add or update its entry in `site/data/labels_and_annotations.yaml` (`key`, `type`, `example`, `used_on`, `description`), saying what sets it, what it does and which feature gate it requires, and matching the actual behaviour. The Labels and Annotations reference page is generated from that file and sorted by key automatically.** Why: about 45 docs requests in about 40 PRs; they also post `/cherry-pick website` themselves (about 340 times). "Extend instead of a new page" was asked once (2026), and in 2025 they asked for a new dedicated concept page instead of growing an index page, so pick the page by topic. The labels file: nothing generates or checks its entries; asked in 3 PRs, 2024–2025 (#3191, #3649, #5215), then in 2026 they found many entries missing (tracking issue #9256) and, in #19278, suggested lines naming the required feature gate and fixes to behaviour descriptions. Evidence:
+  - [PR #13308](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13308#discussion_r4105052844) inline 2026-09-25 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/manage/setup_quota_release_strategy.md:1: "Instead of a dedicated page, can you just add the description to"
+  - [PR #8530](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8530#issuecomment-3745762774) comment 2026-01-13: "could you cherry-pick this to the website as well? It could deliver the FG specification to online documentation."
+  - [PR #5215](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5215#pullrequestreview-2833320565) review 2025-05-12: "Additionally, could you update the labels reference doc?"
+- **[simple] On a docs page for an Alpha or Beta feature, add a `{{% alert title="Note" %}}` card with the feature gate, its stage and the Kueue version that introduced it. Link to the installation feature-gates section instead of repeating how to enable gates.** Why: about 7 PRs, 2024–2025: version alerts in #1705, #1751, #2053 (2024, then titled "Warning"), the Note card in #6259 and #6270, linking to the installation page in #3898 and #7890; the same ask for KEP sections in #12344 (2026). Today's docs pages use this Note form (about 40 such cards). Evidence:
+  - [PR #6259](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6259#discussion_r2241908574) inline 2025-07-30 @ site/content/en/docs/concepts/admission_fair_sharing.md:30: "Could you mention the Kueue version to enable this enhancement as a `alert title="Note" `?"
+  - [PR #7890](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/7890#discussion_r2564283564) inline 2025-11-26 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/manage/enforce_job_management/opt_in_namespace_management.md:88: "We already mention how to enable feature gate in `Before you begin` section."
+- **Write task pages for their reader (batch user, serving user or admin), as step-by-step usage without internal mechanisms. Put full manifests in `site/static/examples/` and embed them.** Why: about 5 PRs, 2024–2025 (#3432, #3454, #3898, #4283). Evidence:
+  - [PR #3898](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/3898#discussion_r1900493945) inline 2025-01-02 @ site/content/en/docs/tasks/run/tektoncd.md:14: "I would like to keep mentioning only end user-facing descriptions here."

+- **[simple] Give every fenced code block in docs and KEPs a language (```` ```yaml ````, ```` ```go ````, ```` ```shell ````).** Why: a small nit in 3 PRs (#1226 2023, #7652 2025, #8861 2026 with 9 suggestions); no markdown linter in the repo checks it. Evidence:
+  <!-- skillsaw-disable-next-line content-tautological -->

+- **[simple] In new generic examples (docs, KEPs, `values.yaml`, config samples, API comments), use neutral placeholders (`example.com/gpu`, `gpu.example.com`, `example.com/sample`) rather than vendor or product names (`nvidia.com/gpu`, MIG), and don't use `kueue.x-k8s.io/...` keys for labels Kueue doesn't provide (mimowo agreed in #5678).** Why: 3 PRs, 2025–2026 (#5372, #5678, #10283). Not absolute: in #10283 (2026-05) their own suggested DRA counter example used the real `gpu.nvidia.com` driver attributes, and today's docs and KEPs still carry about 260 `nvidia.com/gpu` (against about 90 `example.com/gpu`), so expect the ask on new or touched examples. Evidence:

+
+### Logging
+
+- **Pick the verbosity from the intended-use table in Kueue's Logging Policy (`site/content/en/community/contribution_guidelines/logging_policy.md`, since 2026-10): V(1) rare anomalies, V(2) concise summaries, V(3) scheduler cycle progress and main per-object decisions, V(4) decision details, V(5) heap/cache mechanics and other implementation traces. The manager runs at V(2) by default (`--zap-log-level=2`), so keep V(2) to messages worth seeing by default.** Why: operators of large clusters complained about log volume (about 135 entries in about 95 PRs, rising from 2025). Where tenzen-y's older reading of the k8s guide differs, the policy wins: in [PR #16290](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16290) they first asked for V(2) on a "should never happen" message and then agreed with mimowo on V(1), and in [PR #17227](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17227) (2026-10) they argued for V(2) scheduler logs and then accepted V(3). Evidence:
+  - [PR #8432](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8432#discussion_r2655261982) inline 2025-12-31 @ pkg/cache/queue/cluster_queue.go:1: "Could you use `V(5)` for added logs? Because it seems new ones are debug logs, which reveal the internal heap and cache logic."
+  - [PR #9795](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9795#discussion_r2919823066) inline 2026-03-11 @ pkg/scheduler/scheduler.go:213: "the default logs (V2) should be minimized as much as possible."
+  - [PR #16290](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16290#discussion_r4124658776) inline 2026-09-28 @ pkg/scheduler/flavorassigner/flavorassigner.go:273: "In the healthy path, this will never happen. So, we agreed w/ v1."
+- **[simple] Use the logger propagated from the reconcile context, which already carries the request keys, and pass values as structured key/values, not inside the message.** Why: about 8 explicit requests in 7 PRs, 2023–2026. The Logging Policy now says the same. Evidence:
+  - [PR #6325](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6325#discussion_r2245709937) inline 2025-07-31 @ pkg/controller/tas/node_failure_controller.go:331: "We should use the propagated logger from reconciler since it has reconcile request objects."
+  - [PR #4301](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/4301#discussion_r1971661322) inline 2025-02-26 @ pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go:465: "The logger should embed parameters as structured logging args."
+  - [PR #3836](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/3836#discussion_r1884123100) inline 2024-12-13 @ pkg/controller/core/workload_controller.go:611: "It would be better to use structured logging instead of wrapping values inside the message."
+- **[simple] Log Kubernetes objects as `"workload", klog.KObj(wl)` (or `klog.KRef(ns, name)`), with the same lowerCamelCase kind key the other call sites use (`clusterQueue`), not a bare name such as `"workloadName", wl.Name`.** This is for logs only: event and condition messages that other tenants can see keep the UID instead of `namespace/name`, which is PII in multi-tenant clusters (alculquicondor in #1942; tenzen-y had asked for `namespace/name` and accepted). Why: about 9 PRs, 2023–2025 (#561, #578, #1079, #1113, #1313, #1631, #1960, #4300, #5782), almost all bare suggestion blocks. The Coding Guidelines and the Logging Policy say the same. Evidence:
+  - [PR #5782](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5782#discussion_r2216362167) inline 2025-07-18 @ pkg/controller/admissionchecks/multikueue/workload.go:492: ""workload", klog.KObj(group.local)"
+  - [PR #1079](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/1079#discussion_r1320331973) inline 2023-09-08 @ pkg/controller/core/admissioncheck_controller.go:205: "We shouldn't drop the context in logs. Let's keep using "clusterQueue" the same as other locations."
+- **When log levels or keys change, add `ACTION REQUIRED` to the release note. If the change is cherry-picked to a patch release, expect to put it behind a feature gate there.** Why: users' dashboards and log scrapers depend on them (3 PRs, all 2026-10: #17227, #17746, #17747). The patch-release gate comes from #17227 only: mimowo argued logs carry no compatibility promise, then asked the author to add the gate to the cherry-picks. Evidence:
+  - [PR #17227](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17227#issuecomment-6223271518) comment 2026-10-16: "I'm ok w/ cherry picking this to released branch, but it should have a dedicated feature gate since using Loki logging in Grafana dashboard is very common way."
+  - [PR #17746](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17746#pullrequestreview-5654875362) review 2026-10-26: "I also would request ACTION REQUIRED section in release note since we replaced some levels and kv key name"
+
+### Errors
+
+- **[simple] In unit tests, compare errors with `cmp.Diff(tc.wantErr, err, cmpopts.EquateErrors())`: not message strings, not `wantErrSubstring`, not a `wantErr bool` toggle, and always check both want and got. Return sentinel or typed errors so they can be compared. The same goes for events: compare events, not their messages (asked once, 2026). In integration tests, pass a gomega matcher (`utiltesting.BeInvalidError`, `gomega.MatchError(err)`) rather than an expected string.** Why: they call message matching an antipattern (about 20 entries in about 15 PRs, 2023–2026, latest 2026-11; matchers in about 4 PRs, #5268, #13571, #19412). The Coding Guidelines now codify it. Evidence:
+  - [PR #19412](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/19412#discussion_r4679203437) inline 2026-11-24 @ test/integration/singlecluster/webhook/core/capacitybufferconfig_test.go:35: "Can you use a matcher instead of string comparison?"
+  - [PR #12293](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/12293#discussion_r3429256054) inline 2026-06-17 @ pkg/config/config_test.go:935: "This is untipattern. So, can you use error comparision pattern? We can introduce `wantError error` instead of `wantErrSubstring string`."
+  <!-- skillsaw-disable-next-line content-weak-language -->
+  - [PR #15759](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15759#discussion_r4042235453) inline 2026-09-17 @ pkg/workloadslicing/workloadslicing_test.go:893: "We ideally should compare want and got errors using cmpopts.EquateError() instead of boolean toggle."
+  - [PR #6760](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6760#discussion_r2368275241) inline 2025-09-22 @ pkg/controller/jobs/generic/generic_multikueue_adapter_test.go:365: "We should always check want errors and actual errors."
+  - [PR #15829](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15829#discussion_r4105917368) inline 2026-09-25 @ pkg/scheduler/scheduler_test.go:10676: "We should not compare the event message. We should compare events."
+- **Check nil paths that really panic: writing into a nil map (for example `maps.Copy` into nil `Labels`), and indexing a nil slice where a map used to be.** Reading a key from a nil map is safe. They accepted this when shown, as in PR #2813 about `GetLabels()`, so don't add guards for plain map reads. Why: about 15 inline entries in about 13 PRs, 2023–2025. Evidence:
+  - [PR #5859](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5859#discussion_r2182016774) inline 2025-07-03 @ pkg/controller/jobs/pod/pod_controller.go:1055: "The `maps.Copy` does not check if the dst map (`wl.Labels`) is nil."
+  - [PR #6648](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6648#discussion_r2341236322) inline 2025-09-11 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/tas_flavor_snapshot.go:513: "But, the new field is slice which could not be accessible when slice is null."
+
+### API and feature gates
+
+- **Guard all new Alpha logic behind a feature gate, with one gate check covering all the new blocks.** Why: a feature that has a gate can be switched off when a critical bug shows up (about 26 entries in 22 PRs, 2023–2026). Evidence:
+  - [PR #17781](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17781#discussion_r4645895869) inline 2026-11-20 @ cmd/kueue/main.go:605: "To clarify, all new alpha logic should be guarded by a feature gate."
+  - [PR #6210](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6210#discussion_r2478721513) inline 2025-10-30 @ keps/3258-delayed-admission-check-retries/kep.yaml:29: "Shouldn't we introduce FG so that we can immediately disable this feature once we observe critical bugs?"
+- **Don't request backports of enhancements, non-Alpha API or config-schema changes, or feature-stage changes: they stay on main, and a patch release must not change anything users of non-Alpha features rely on. Bug fixes and minor improvements to Alpha features may be backported (`RELEASE.md`). Cleanups usually stay on main too, but they do cherry-pick risk-free test fixes and Helm chart cleanups to keep the branches aligned. Behaviour-changing fixes that get backported ship as Alpha, disabled by default, on release branches (not "Beta, disabled"), while main may use Beta, enabled. Use the k/k meaning of Alpha and Beta.** Why: the stage convention: about 6 entries in 5 PRs, all 2026-08/09, described as the project convention. The scope: about 5 threads, 2024–2026 (#1585, #9534, issue #15564, #15633, #17227; `RELEASE.md` follows the k8s cherry-pick principles, "more relaxed" for Alpha). In #15633 mimowo had already queued the cherry-picks and dropped them after this comment. The cleanup exceptions: #5940 ("Generally, the cleanup PR will not be cherry-picked"), against their own cherry-picks of a test fix (#7752) and a `kind/cleanup` Helm RBAC change (#14816); mimowo also cherry-picks test-only and mechanical refactor PRs. Evidence:
+  - [PR #13797](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13797#issuecomment-5355108806) comment 2026-08-20: "Those cherry picks should have "0.18, Alpha, disabled by default" and "0.19, Alpha, disabled by default" changes."
+  - [PR #13749](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/13749#discussion_r3736959678) inline 2026-08-07 @ pkg/webhooks/workload_webhook.go:61: "Beta API means disabling by default and Beta Feature means enabling by default in k/k."
+  - [PR #15633](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15633#issuecomment-5705331482) comment 2026-09-16: "This is an enhancement, not a bug fix. So, we shouldn't backport it to the released branches."
+  - [PR #9534](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9534#issuecomment-3967905680) comment 2026-02-26: "In general, we don't cherry-pick such non-alpha-level API changes to the released version, which might break the user's production."
+  - [PR #14816](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14816#issuecomment-5429453158) comment 2026-08-26: "But, we can backport this PR as same as previous Helm chart cleanup PRs regardless of PR kind."
+- **Write the feature-gate entry in `pkg/features/kube_features.go` this way. Under `// owner:`, link the KEP (`// kep:`) or the tracking issue (`// issue:`), not the PR (`// pr:`, still on 14 older entries). `Version` is the first release that ships the gate, counting backports. When the gate starts as Beta on main, omit the Alpha entry there and add the Alpha entry in the cherry-picks only. A deprecated gate gets `LockToDefault: true` and a `// remove in 0.X` comment (the gate case of the `TODO` rule under Comments and docs).** Why: about 13 entries in 13 PRs, 2026 (#8568, #9532, #10384, #11255, #14128, #14682, #14734, #15262). Evidence:
+  - [PR #10384](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10384#discussion_r3071580259) inline 2026-04-13 @ pkg/features/kube_features.go:358: "Could you use the tracking issue?"
+  - [PR #9532](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9532#discussion_r2862297952) inline 2026-02-27 @ pkg/features/kube_features.go:287: "Shouldn't we make this 0.15? Because we cherry-picked this FG into release v0.15 as well"
+  - [PR #11255](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/11255#pullrequestreview-4311736017) review 2026-05-18: "Can you lock deprecated FG and add a comment for the removal version?"
+- **[simple] In tests, enable a feature gate only in the cases that need it, not for the whole suite. Keep tests for both gate states while the gate is Beta. Don't create test functions dedicated to a gate: name the test after the behaviour and cover the gate states as cases.** Why: about 6 entries in 5 PRs, 2023–2026; `writing_tests.md` also asks for both gate states. Evidence:
+  - [PR #6973](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/6973#discussion_r2375824093) inline 2025-09-24 @ test/integration/singlecluster/scheduler/suite_test.go:68: "We should avoid enabling such Alpha feature at global level. We should set the FG only in the affected case."
+  - [PR #9450](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9450#pullrequestreview-3848526893) review 2026-02-24: "Could you keep featureGates enabling / disabling tests because this FG is still Beta?"
+  - [PR #14203](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14203#discussion_r3791976549) inline 2026-08-16 @ pkg/cache/scheduler/tas_nodes_cache_test.go:276: "We don't want to maintain UTs only for FG."
+- **Add API fields only when they are needed, and prefer internal mechanisms. A field cannot be removed without a version change, so deprecate rather than remove. Don't add a field, label or condition that repeats information the object already holds (`.status.clusterName`, `.status.nominatedClusterNames`, the `QuotaReserved` or `Finished` conditions, `spec.priorityClassRef`): reuse or extend the existing one, so one field owns each fact.** Why: about 6 entries in 5 PRs, 2024–2026; duplicated information in 2 more PRs, 2026 (#10266 with 4 threads, #14554; the duplicate-timestamp case in the next rule is the same idea), since two sources for one fact drift apart. This applies when the existing field already holds the same fact: in #5127 (2025) their "why can we not reuse `requeueState`" was answered by mimowo (it holds no reason and counts other requeues too), and Kueue added `status.schedulingStats.evictions` instead. Evidence:
+  - [PR #5981](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5981#discussion_r2293140047) inline 2025-08-22 @ keps/2349-multikueue-external-custom-job-support/README.md:118: "The API has highly maintainance costs since we can NOT break that w/o API version change"
+  - [PR #10266](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10266#discussion_r3111863454) inline 2026-04-20 @ keps/10189-multikueue-global-status-condition/README.md:70: "Why do we need this? This is obviously duplicated with `.status.clusterName`."
+  - [PR #14554](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14554#discussion_r4042307534) inline 2026-09-17 @ keps/13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md:418: "If we propagate it to Workload, the Workload will have 2 priority class specificatioins. The priority class should be handled only by one field."
+- **Follow the k8s API conventions: object references end in `Ref`, names stay consistent with sibling fields (`...State`, `...Strategy`, `...Policy`), and object state goes into `status.conditions` (type, status, reason, `lastTransitionTime`), not a `phase` enum. Don't add a separate timestamp that duplicates a condition's transition time; a dedicated time field is fine when it tracks updates or renewals that are not status transitions, and the KEP should then say why `lastTransitionTime` can't serve. Values that kueue-controller-manager computes go in `status`: each `spec` has one writer (a human or one dedicated controller), so Kueue must not rewrite a user-owned spec such as ClusterQueue quotas. Design the `status` field from the first Alpha; they accepted spec writes in an Alpha only as a compromise, with the `status` field required in the next Alpha (today's `Workload.spec.active` is a known exception they call "the same problem"; don't copy it).** Why: `Ref` asked in 4 PRs and sibling consistency in about 5 PRs, 2025–2026. Phase: 1 issue (#1790, 2024, citing the API conventions). Duplicate timestamp: #14104 (2026-08), where `lastUpdateTime` was removed in favour of a condition. They accepted dedicated times in three other cases: the AFS `LastUpdate` in #4252 (mwielgus: "Conditions have last transition, not last update"; it ships in today's LocalQueue status), the preemption-limit window `lastUpdateTime` in #17277, and `validUntil` in #18111 (2026-11, where they asked for an Alternatives section explaining why conditions don't fit). Spec writer: 3 PRs, 2025–2026 (#5410, #10145, #16088; cycle 2 dropped it at 2). In #10145 they accepted mimowo's plan (Alpha1 writes the ClusterQueue spec, Alpha2 moves to `status`) if the team "really should rush this", with the status field a "MUST-HAVE for graduation". KEP-9988 Alpha1 shipped that way in v0.18. KEP-17401 replaced it: DQO writes `ClusterQueue.status.effectiveQuotas`, and Kueue no longer changes the spec. Evidence:
+  - [PR #7570](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/7570#discussion_r2543766166) inline 2025-11-19 @ keps/693-multikueue/README.md:207: "This is a typical object reference pattern which should have a `ref` suffix."
+  - [PR #5211](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/5211#discussion_r2084584113) inline 2025-05-12 @ keps/1282-pods-ready-requeue-strategy/README.md:211: "Let's use the `evictionState` to align with other fields like `requeueState` and `admisssionCheckState`."
+  - [PR #14104](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14104#discussion_r3847556592) inline 2026-08-24 @ keps/12382-dynamic-quota-orchestration/README.md:537: "We should track this time in conditions[*].lastTransitionTime."
+  - [Issue #1790](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/1790#issuecomment-1976361978) comment 2024-03-04: "According to the API design docs, introducing the `phase` field would be deprecated and not recommended."
+  - [PR #10145](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10145#discussion_r3210512787) inline 2026-05-08 @ keps/9988-multikueue-manager-quota-automation/README.md:348: "TBH, even in the first alpha phase, we should not modify the cluster queue spec field."
+  - [PR #16088](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/16088#discussion_r4406042101) inline 2026-10-28 @ keps/10270-local-capacity-provider/README.md:479: "The spec writer should be unique, like a human or a dedicated controller."
+- **Design condition types and reasons around the existing state machine. Don't add a feature-specific condition type that splits an existing state (a MultiKueue-only condition next to `Finished` splits the terminal state into two types); extend the existing condition's reasons instead (`SucceededOnMultiKueueWorkerCluster`). When a new reason narrows an existing one, reuse the existing reason (`WaitingForQuota`) or express the cause as `<Reason>DueTo<Cause>` (`ExceedsMaxQuotaDueToBorrowingLimitReached`, `DeactivatedDueToXYZ`), not as a second overlapping reason. A reason says in machine-readable form why the condition has its status, without repeating the condition type (`Evicted` with reason `NodeFailures`, not `WorkloadEvictedDueToNodeFailures`).** A single positive reason such as `Ready` on `Active=True` is still an open choice: in #18111 they proposed `Issued`/`Renewed` instead of `Ready`, mimowo preferred one positive reason as in `ClusterQueueActive` (which uses `Ready` today), and both accepted `Issued`/`Renewed` for Alpha only, to revisit before Beta. Why: Kueue acts on `status.conditions`, so extra types and overlapping reasons make the state machine harder to keep consistent. About 5 threads, 2025–2026 (#5272, issue #6359, #10266, #11720, #18111). In #5272 mimowo agreed ("the condition type already sets the context"), and in #11720 mimowo and tenzen-y settled on reusing `WaitingForQuota`. Today's code follows it (`WorkloadEvictedDueToNodeFailures = "NodeFailures"`, `%sDueTo%s` in `pkg/workload/patching/patching.go`); `coding_guidelines.md` only asks for PascalCase reasons. Evidence:
+  - [PR #11720](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/11720#discussion_r3404486371) inline 2026-06-12 @ keps/10852-inadmissible-workloads-observability/README.md:266: "If we want to tell this "borrowing" based reasons, shouldn't we handle this as underlying cause like "ExceedsMaxQuotaDueToBorrowingLimitReached"?"
+  - [PR #18111](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/18111#discussion_r4635590127) inline 2026-11-19 @ keps/12382-dynamic-quota-orchestration/README.md:410: "The condition reason should describe "why" this Active=True as machine readable shape."
+  - [PR #10266](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10266#discussion_r3190391844) inline 2026-05-05 @ keps/10189-multikueue-global-status-condition/README.md:71: "I'm worried about adding a new condition type only for MultiKueue because Workloads could be in an unstable phase, which means the Workload terminal state will be split into 2 condition types."
+- **Mark every API field `+optional` or `+required`, and prefer required when the field always has a value, since a required field can be relaxed later. Optional fields get `omitempty` (and a pointer when the zero value means something). For `omitempty` on required fields, follow the tooling: in the CRD APIs (`apis/kueue/v1beta2`, `v1alpha1`) `make lint-api` (KAL `optionalorrequired`, `optionalfields`, `requiredfields`, enabled 2025-11) checks markers, pointers and tags, and its `requiredfields` check expects `omitempty` unless the field is in the `.golangci-kal.yaml` exclusion list. In the Config API (`apis/config`, not linted by KAL) required fields drop `omitempty`.** Why: about 50 entries in 35 PRs, 2022–2026 (for example #4337, #4543, #4687, #5203, #8861, #14557, #16300, #17019, #17277). Their 2025 "drop `omitempty` for required fields" asks (#4543, #4687, #5203) predate the linter; today about 70 of 110 required CRD fields keep `omitempty`, and in #7796 (2025-11) they removed it from one field by adding that field to the exclusion list. In 2026 their asks are about the required/optional choice itself. Evidence:
+  - [PR #4337](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/4337#discussion_r1985492805) inline 2025-03-07 @ apis/config/v1beta1/configuration_types.go:173: "Could you add `+optional` marker to all optional fields?"
+  - [PR #4687](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/4687#discussion_r2078578829) inline 2025-05-07 @ apis/config/v1beta1/configuration_types.go:1: "Could we drop `omitempty` tags for required fields?"
+  - [PR #14557](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14557#discussion_r3914499382) inline 2026-09-02 @ apis/kueue/v1alpha1/capacityprovider_types.go:102: "Isn't this required?"
+  - [PR #17019](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17019#discussion_r4339734759) inline 2026-10-21 @ keps/13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md:947: "Because we can relax these required validations afterwards."
+- **Start new API validation strict, since relaxing it later is compatible but tightening it breaks stored objects: small `MaxItems` and caps (16 rather than 64, a 10-minute cap rather than none), and reject values the first iteration doesn't support (Kueue-side validation even where the API server checks the label or Job). When you raise a limit, bring scale evidence (a real use case, benchmark results) or document the performance caveat, and put any tightening of an already released validation behind a feature gate.** Why: extends the "required can be relaxed later" point of the rule above to limits and unsupported values. About 8 threads, 2025-10 to 2026-11 (#7283, issue #8561, #8861, #14554, #15262, #17019, #19236, #19311). In #19236 they removed their LGTM until the team shared benchmark results, but in issue #8561 they agreed to raise 10 to 20 clusters without benchmarks, with a performance note in the docs, and in #19236 mimowo argued that performance should not block raising a limit ("allow for a bit more than we are comfortable with"), so don't treat benchmarks as a hard prerequisite. In #14104 they asked to reject unsupported quota fields and then withdrew it once the author explained the fields were copied from spec. Evidence:
+  - [PR #8861](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/8861#discussion_r3056368017) inline 2026-04-09 @ keps/8691-concurrent-admission/README.md:439: "Because if we start with 64 limitations in alpha, we can never restrict this limitation, even if we face any performance regressions."
+  - [PR #17019](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/17019#discussion_r4283918108) inline 2026-10-15 @ keps/13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md:871: "Is this 32 limitation based on a real use case? If not, I'd propose narrowing it down to 16 or fewer values to keep this API safe."
+  - [PR #14554](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/14554#discussion_r4046475440) inline 2026-09-18 @ keps/13396-configurable-preemptions/README.md:765: "Yes, non effective value should be rejected. Once we support negative value, we can relax the validation."
+  - [PR #15262](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/15262#issuecomment-5720182882) review 2026-09-17: "Because this makes the released validation rules strong which cause running workload disruptions."
+- **[simple] Prefix new API Go type names with their parent kind (`PreemptionConfigPreemptionRule`, `MultiKueueConfig...`, `PodSetSlice...`). All CRD types share one `apis` package, so generic names collide.** Why: about 10 PRs, 2024–2026 (#3013, #5149, #5211, #5290, #7317, #9243, #10145, #10283, #14104, #14554). Evidence:
+  - [PR #10145](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/10145#discussion_r3235091652) inline 2026-05-13 @ keps/9988-multikueue-manager-quota-automation/README.md:371: "can you add `MulitKueueConfig` prefix since this typed name is shared within all our APIs."
+  - [PR #9243](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/pull/9243#discussion_r2819028139) inline 2026-02-17 @ keps/2724-topology-aware-scheduling/README.md:986: "to avoid typed name collisions across CRDs"
+- **[simple] Write the default value in the API field comment. For static defaults use `+kubebuilder:default` rather than webhook or controller code; dynamic defaults go in the defaulting webhook.** Why: about 6 PRs, 2023–2026: the default in the comment in #1251 and #13308, schema defaults over webhook code in #849, #1637 and #17012, dynamic defaults in the webhook in #3375. Evidence:
```

  Fixed in 196289d00. The rule now limits `+kubebuilder:default` to CRD APIs and says Config API defaults go in `apis/config/v1beta2/defaults.go` (`SetDefaults_Configuration`), since the Config API takes no kubebuilder markers.
